### Vibe Modeling: Challenges and Opportunities
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23120v1)
- **Authors**: Jordi Cabot
- **Abstract**: There is a pressing need for better development methods and tools to keep up with the growing demand and increasing complexity of new software systems. New types of user interfaces, the need for intelligent components, sustainability concerns, ... bring new challenges that we need to handle. In the last years, model-driven engineering (MDE) has been key to improving the quality and productivity of software development, but models themselves are becoming increasingly complex to specify and manage. At the same time, we are witnessing the growing popularity of vibe coding approaches that rely on Large Language Models (LLMs) to transform natural language descriptions into running code at the expenses of code vulnerabilities, scalability issues and maintainability concerns. In this paper, we introduce the concept of \textit{vibe modeling} as a novel approach to integrate the best of both worlds (AI and MDE) to speed up the development of reliable complex systems. We outline the key concepts of vibe modeling and highlight the opportunities and open challenges it presents for the future of modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Vibe Modeling: Challenges and Opportunities":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the concept of "vibe modeling," which aims to combine the benefits of AI/LLMs and Model-Driven Engineering (MDE) for software development. It proposes a development process where natural language descriptions are transformed into models through conversational interaction with an LLM-based agent. These models are then used to generate code using traditional MDE techniques, promising more reliable and understandable outputs compared to direct code generation from LLMs ("vibe coding"). The paper discusses the advantages, necessary infrastructure (using the Model Context Protocol - MCP), and open challenges of this approach, including specialized modeling agents, handling uncertainty, adapting to different user profiles, and integrating agents into low-code platforms. It further explores collaborative vibe modeling, involving multiple specialized agents working together.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The central idea of combining LLMs and MDE through an interactive, model-centric approach possesses a moderate degree of novelty.  Existing works focus largely on one-shot model inference from natural language, whereas this paper promotes a more iterative and conversational process.  The proposal to leverage MCP for standardizing agent-modeling platform interaction is also a practical and somewhat novel suggestion. However, the core principle of using models as a bridge between high-level descriptions and code generation is not entirely new, as it lies at the heart of MDE itself. The primary novelty resides in the specific integration of LLMs within that established framework. The application of the agentic paradigm in modeling is an area that is only now beginning to be explored, and thus is where much of the novelty rests.

**Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to address key limitations of both vibe coding and traditional MDE. Vibe coding suffers from issues of reliability, maintainability, and explainability. MDE, while offering these qualities, can be perceived as complex and time-consuming. Vibe modeling aims to strike a balance, streamlining the initial development stages using LLMs while retaining the benefits of model-driven generation for correctness and maintainability.

However, the paper remains largely conceptual.  It lacks concrete implementation details, empirical evaluation, and comparisons against existing approaches.  The challenges identified are valid, but the paper doesn't offer substantial solutions or demonstrate the feasibility of overcoming them. For instance, the paper highlights challenges in AI model construction (e.g. biases). Similarly, the discussion regarding training the AI agents only gives a brief overview.

The reliance on MCP is a practical consideration, but also adds complexity to the infrastructure, and the benefit may be limited by the complexity of the models. Furthermore, the benefit of collaborative vibe modeling remains to be demonstrated.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition:**  The paper effectively identifies the limitations of current software development approaches and the need for a more robust solution.
*   **Well-defined concept:** The concept of vibe modeling is presented clearly and logically.
*   **Practical recommendations:** The suggestion to use MCP for agent-platform interaction is valuable and directly applicable.
*   **Comprehensive overview of challenges:** The paper acknowledges and discusses a wide range of challenges related to vibe modeling, covering both technical and user-related aspects.
*   **Addresses a timely topic:** Considering the increasing prevalence of LLMs in software development, the paper contributes to a vital discussion about responsible and effective integration.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of implementation and evaluation:**  The paper is largely theoretical and lacks any empirical evaluation or implementation details to support its claims.
*   **Limited depth of solutions:** While challenges are well-defined, the paper offers only high-level suggestions for addressing them, lacking concrete strategies.
*   **Unclear benefit of collaborative vibe modeling:** While proposed, the added complexity and practical implementation of collaborative vibe modeling seems unclear and has no evidence of potential efficacy.
*   **Overreliance on existing concepts:** Relies strongly on existing concepts and fails to elaborate on how each of the challenges will specifically be addressed when they are combined into a single paradigm.

**Potential Influence:** The paper can stimulate further research in the area of AI-assisted modeling and guide the development of new tools and methodologies. It also has the potential to influence the direction of low-code platforms towards more structured and reliable AI integration. However, its impact depends on future research efforts to validate its concepts and address the identified challenges.

**Score: 5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a conceptually sound and timely idea – vibe modeling. It effectively bridges the gap between the automated code generation of LLMs and the reliable structure of MDE. However, the lack of concrete implementation, empirical validation, and in-depth solutions to the identified challenges significantly limits its current impact. The ideas put forth by the paper are good stepping stones for future researchers, but not significant contributions on their own. Furthermore, several aspects of the paper (e.g. the additional benefit of collaborative vibe modeling) require additional evidence to be considered valid. Overall, the paper's novelty and potential significance are promising, but its lack of practical demonstration warrants a moderate score.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 5/10

### Uncovering the Fragility of Trustworthy LLMs through Chinese Textual Ambiguity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23121v1)
- **Authors**: Xinwei Wu, Haojie Li, Hongyu Liu, Xinyu Ji, Ruohan Li, Yule Chen, Yigeng Zhang
- **Abstract**: In this work, we study a critical research problem regarding the trustworthiness of large language models (LLMs): how LLMs behave when encountering ambiguous narrative text, with a particular focus on Chinese textual ambiguity. We created a benchmark dataset by collecting and generating ambiguous sentences with context and their corresponding disambiguated pairs, representing multiple possible interpretations. These annotated examples are systematically categorized into 3 main categories and 9 subcategories. Through experiments, we discovered significant fragility in LLMs when handling ambiguity, revealing behavior that differs substantially from humans. Specifically, LLMs cannot reliably distinguish ambiguous text from unambiguous text, show overconfidence in interpreting ambiguous text as having a single meaning rather than multiple meanings, and exhibit overthinking when attempting to understand the various possible meanings. Our findings highlight a fundamental limitation in current LLMs that has significant implications for their deployment in real-world applications where linguistic ambiguity is common, calling for improved approaches to handle uncertainty in language understanding. The dataset and code are publicly available at this GitHub repository: https://github.com/ictup/LLM-Chinese-Textual-Disambiguation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the trustworthiness of Large Language Models (LLMs) when processing ambiguous Chinese text. The authors created a new benchmark dataset of ambiguous Chinese sentences, categorized into lexical, syntactic, and semantic-pragmatic ambiguity types, along with disambiguated versions. They conducted experiments using various open-weight LLMs to assess their ability to detect ambiguity, interpret it (generate multiple possible meanings), and generate disambiguated sentences. The results revealed that LLMs often struggle with ambiguity, exhibiting overconfidence in single interpretations, failure to reliably detect ambiguity, and overthinking in explanation attempts. The paper concludes that current LLMs have limitations in handling linguistic ambiguity, hindering their deployment in real-world applications.  The authors also explore various prompting techniques, including RAG and CoT, to mitigate the effects of ambiguity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in creating a new Chinese-specific benchmark dataset for textual ambiguity and using this dataset to rigorously evaluate the performance of several open-weight LLMs across several tasks (ambiguity detection, interpretation, and resolution). While the concept of testing LLMs with ambiguous inputs isn't entirely new, the focus on Chinese language and the systematic categorization of ambiguity types adds a significant dimension. Prior works, such as the AMBIENT dataset mentioned, focused on English. The experiments are also thoroughly documented using various prompting strategies, which further strengthens the contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a critical aspect of LLM trustworthiness: how they handle uncertainty and ambiguity. If LLMs are to be deployed in real-world applications where human-like language use is essential, their ability to handle the inherent ambiguity of language is paramount. The paper convincingly demonstrates that existing LLMs are often fragile in the face of ambiguity, raising concerns about their reliability in critical applications like customer service, legal document analysis, or medical diagnosis. The identification of specific failure modes (overconfidence, overthinking) is valuable for future research aimed at improving LLM robustness. Furthermore, the open-sourcing of the dataset is a significant contribution enabling other researchers to test other models and methodologies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The creation and open-sourcing of the Chinese textual ambiguity dataset is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Systematic Experimentation:** The paper presents a well-designed experimental setup with a variety of LLMs, prompting techniques (including RAG and CoT), and evaluation metrics.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making the findings easy to understand.
    *   **Real-world Relevance:** The study is highly relevant to the practical deployment of LLMs and highlights a potential source of errors in real-world applications.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of the experimental results, identifying specific failure modes and discussing the effectiveness of different prompting strategies.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:**  While focused on the Chinese language, the ambiguity phenomena discussed have counterparts in other languages.  While this paper makes a convincing case for Chinese textual ambiguity, the extent to which the presented dataset and findings are applicable to LLMs handling ambiguity in other languages (English, Spanish etc) is not explored.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The accuracy, precision, recall, and F1 metrics used primarily reflect alignment with human annotations, which, while practical, is not presented as an objective determination of the ambiguity of a sentence. The study acknowledges this, but acknowledging doesn't fully mitigate the limitation. More nuanced methods for evaluating the quality of generated interpretations could have been considered.
    *   **Specific Model Focus:** The study relies primarily on open-weight models. Future work could consider proprietary models for a more complete assessment, although the choice of open-weight is understandable for transparency.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It provides a benchmark dataset for evaluating LLMs on Chinese textual ambiguity.
    *   It highlights the need for improved techniques for handling ambiguity in LLMs.
    *   It inspires the development of more robust LLMs that are less susceptible to ambiguity-related errors.
    *   It provides guidelines for using prompting techniques to mitigate the effects of ambiguity.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution by creating a Chinese-specific ambiguity benchmark and using it to demonstrate the limitations of existing LLMs in handling ambiguity. The systematic experiments, clear presentation, and open-sourcing of the dataset are all valuable strengths. The paper is a practical resource that highlights an important problem that may have been overlooked. While it has some limitations in terms of generalizability and evaluation metrics, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses.

### ISO-Bench: Benchmarking Multimodal Causal Reasoning in Visual-Language Models through Procedural Plans
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23135v1)
- **Authors**: Ananya Sadana, Yash Kumar Lal, Jiawei Zhou
- **Abstract**: Understanding causal relationships across modalities is a core challenge for multimodal models operating in real-world environments. We introduce ISO-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating whether models can infer causal dependencies between visual observations and procedural text. Each example presents an image of a task step and a text snippet from a plan, with the goal of deciding whether the visual step occurs before or after the referenced text step. Evaluation results on ten frontier vision-language models show underwhelming performance: the best zero-shot F1 is only 0.57, and chain-of-thought reasoning yields only modest gains (up to 0.62 F1), largely behind humans (0.98 F1). Our analysis further highlights concrete directions for improving causal understanding in multimodal models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ISO-BENCH, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the ability of multimodal visual-language models (VLMs) to reason about causal dependencies between visual observations and procedural text.  The benchmark consists of examples drawn from instructional videos, where the task is to determine whether an image depicting a step in a procedure occurs before or after a referenced textual step in the plan.  The paper evaluates several state-of-the-art VLMs on this benchmark and finds that their performance is surprisingly poor, even with chain-of-thought reasoning. The authors analyze the errors made by the models and highlight areas for improvement in multimodal causal understanding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a valuable contribution by highlighting a critical gap in the capabilities of current VLMs: the ability to reason about causal relationships across modalities within the context of procedural plans. While existing benchmarks address visual question answering or plan reasoning separately, ISO-BENCH directly tests the *integration* of visual and textual information for causal inference. The dataset construction by leveraging existing instructional video datasets is a reasonable and pragmatic approach. However, while the concept itself is valuable, the method to create the dataset (random pairing within a certain distance of steps) is quite simple and can lead to less challenging negative samples.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed by ISO-BENCH is highly relevant to real-world applications of AI, such as embodied AI, robot navigation, and assistive technologies. The poor performance of current VLMs on this task underscores the need for further research in this area. The error analysis provides valuable insights into the limitations of current models, which can guide future research directions. The creation of the benchmark itself is a tangible contribution that will enable researchers to develop and evaluate new approaches for multimodal causal reasoning.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper identifies a clear and important problem in multimodal reasoning.
    *   ISO-BENCH provides a concrete testbed for evaluating progress in this area.
    *   The paper analyzes model errors, revealing specific weaknesses in causal understanding, grounding, and action progression.
    *   The benchmark is constructed from realistic, real-world instructional videos.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance of the models could be considered quite low, making the benchmark relatively easy to saturate for future, more advanced models.
    *   The dataset creation methodology, while pragmatic, is relatively simplistic (random pairing within a distance), which may not fully capture the complexity of real-world causal relationships.
    *   The error analysis could be expanded to include more detailed qualitative examples.
    *   The paper primarily focuses on English-language documents.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper will likely influence research in the following ways:
    *   It will stimulate the development of new models and algorithms for multimodal causal reasoning.
    *   It will encourage researchers to consider the importance of temporal and causal dependencies in plan understanding.
    *   ISO-BENCH will become a standard benchmark for evaluating progress in this area.
    *   The error analysis will guide the design of more effective training strategies and model architectures.

**Overall Assessment:**

ISO-BENCH is a valuable addition to the field of multimodal reasoning. Although the dataset creation method is relatively simple, and the initial performance is low, it addresses a critical gap in existing benchmarks and provides a concrete challenge for future research. The error analysis contributes actionable insights for improving model capabilities. The paper presents a well-defined problem with solid analysis.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a significant contribution by highlighting a critical gap in the ability of VLMs to perform causal reasoning in multimodal contexts and by introducing the ISO-BENCH benchmark. The importance and the potential impact of the presented research justifies this score. While the experimental results of the tested models are less impressive given the low accuracy, and some aspects of the benchmark construction and analysis could be enhanced, the introduction of a dataset and the highlight of an overlooked task is worth the 7, based on solid and thorough argumentation.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### X-NeMo: Expressive Neural Motion Reenactment via Disentangled Latent Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23143v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaochen Zhao, Hongyi Xu, Guoxian Song, You Xie, Chenxu Zhang, Xiu Li, Linjie Luo, Jinli Suo, Yebin Liu
- **Abstract**: We propose X-NeMo, a novel zero-shot diffusion-based portrait animation pipeline that animates a static portrait using facial movements from a driving video of a different individual. Our work first identifies the root causes of the key issues in prior approaches, such as identity leakage and difficulty in capturing subtle and extreme expressions. To address these challenges, we introduce a fully end-to-end training framework that distills a 1D identity-agnostic latent motion descriptor from driving image, effectively controlling motion through cross-attention during image generation. Our implicit motion descriptor captures expressive facial motion in fine detail, learned end-to-end from a diverse video dataset without reliance on pretrained motion detectors. We further enhance expressiveness and disentangle motion latents from identity cues by supervising their learning with a dual GAN decoder, alongside spatial and color augmentations. By embedding the driving motion into a 1D latent vector and controlling motion via cross-attention rather than additive spatial guidance, our design eliminates the transmission of spatial-aligned structural clues from the driving condition to the diffusion backbone, substantially mitigating identity leakage. Extensive experiments demonstrate that X-NeMo surpasses state-of-the-art baselines, producing highly expressive animations with superior identity resemblance. Our code and models are available for research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "X-NEMO: EXPRESSIVE NEURAL Motion REENACTMENT VIA DISENTANGLED LATENT ATTENTION" introduces a new diffusion-based portrait animation framework that aims to improve the expressiveness and identity consistency of animated portraits generated from a single reference image and a driving video. The core innovation is the use of a 1D identity-agnostic latent motion descriptor extracted from the driving video and integrated into the diffusion backbone using cross-attention mechanisms. The method also employs a dual GAN decoder and various training strategies like color/spatial augmentations and reference feature masking to further disentangle motion from identity and improve the quality of expressions. The paper demonstrates that X-NeMo outperforms existing methods in terms of both quantitative metrics and qualitative visual results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper exhibits good novelty in several aspects. The primary innovation lies in the structure-agnostic motion control using a 1D latent vector integrated via cross-attention. This is a significant departure from prior approaches that often rely on 2D spatial guidance methods like ControlNet, which can lead to identity leakage. The end-to-end training of the motion encoder alongside the diffusion model, without relying on pre-trained motion detectors, is also a noteworthy contribution. The strategies designed to help disentanglement of identity and motion are also reasonably novel, since the authors are directly addressing problems identified with previous approaches.

* **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from addressing key limitations of existing portrait animation techniques, namely identity leakage and limited expressiveness. By achieving better disentanglement and more accurate motion transfer, the authors have potentially advanced the state-of-the-art in zero-shot portrait reenactment. The ability to generate more realistic and nuanced animations has applications in areas such as video conferencing, visual effects, and the creation of digital avatars. The extensive ablations studies that rigorously prove the effectiveness of the proposed components also adds significance to the work.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Quantitative and Qualitative Results:** The paper presents compelling evidence that X-NeMo surpasses existing methods in terms of both identity preservation and expression accuracy.
    * **Well-Designed Architecture:** The architectural design is well-motivated and specifically addresses the identified challenges. The cross-attention mechanism is a key component for achieving identity-disentanglement.
    * **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies demonstrate the effectiveness of each component of the framework, further supporting the claims of the paper.
    * **Applications:**  The paper also highlights potential applications of the proposed method, such as video outpainting and latent motion interpolation.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Cost:** The paper mentions the computational cost of inference (24 GB memory and ~20 seconds per second of video). While this is typical of diffusion-based methods, it may limit the practicality of the approach for real-time applications.
    * **Out-of-Domain Performance:**  The limitations section acknowledges that the method is trained on real human faces and may exhibit artifacts when applied to stylized or non-human characters. This is a common limitation in many deep learning systems but is a crucial aspect that is not addressed for future work.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper is likely to have a moderate to high impact on the field of portrait animation and potentially broader areas of image and video generation.  The demonstrated improvements in identity consistency and expression transfer could lead to further research in disentangled representation learning and cross-modal transfer. It can serve as a solid foundation to continue the development of robust zero-shot animators.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a clear contribution by addressing a relevant and challenging problem in portrait animation. The authors propose a novel architecture and training strategies that demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods. The strengths include a well-motivated design, comprehensive experiments, and promising qualitative results. While the computational cost is a concern, it is an inherent trade-off in many diffusion-based methods. Taking this into account, the paper deserves a solid score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LENS: Learning Ensemble Confidence from Neural States for Multi-LLM Answer Integration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23167v1)
- **Authors**: Jizhou Guo
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive performance across various tasks, with different models excelling in distinct domains and specific abilities. Effectively combining the predictions of multiple LLMs is crucial for enhancing system robustness and performance. However, existing ensemble methods often rely on simple techniques like voting or logits ensembling, which overlook the varying confidence and reliability of models in different contexts. In this work, we propose LENS (Learning ENsemble confidence from Neural States), a novel approach that learns to estimate model confidence by analyzing internal representations. For each LLM, we train a lightweight linear confidence predictor that leverages layer-wise hidden states and normalized probabilities as inputs. This allows for more nuanced weighting of model predictions based on their context-dependent reliability. Our method does not require modifying the model parameters and requires negligible additional computation. Experimental results on multiple-choice and boolean question-answering tasks demonstrate that LENS outperforms traditional ensemble methods by a substantial margin. Our findings suggest that internal representations provide valuable signals for determining model confidence and can be effectively leveraged for ensemble learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LENS (Learning Ensemble confidence from Neural States), a novel method for ensembling Large Language Models (LLMs). Unlike traditional ensemble techniques that rely on simple averaging or voting, LENS learns to estimate the confidence of each LLM's prediction based on its internal representations (hidden states and normalized probabilities from different layers). A lightweight linear confidence predictor is trained for each LLM, and the final prediction is selected from the model with the highest predicted confidence. Experiments on multiple-choice and boolean question-answering tasks demonstrate that LENS outperforms conventional ensemble methods.  The core idea is to leverage the rich information within LLM hidden states to better weight the contributions of each model in an ensemble, leading to more robust and accurate predictions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the way confidence is estimated and used for ensembling. While using internal representations of neural networks isn't entirely new (e.g., logit lens), applying it to *ensemble confidence estimation* specifically and combining it with a simple, lightweight linear model is a worthwhile contribution.  The paper effectively explores a relatively untapped area in LLM ensembling. Traditional methods tend to treat LLMs as black boxes, but LENS looks *inside* to guide the ensembling process.

* **Significance:** The significance is primarily in its practical improvements to LLM ensembling. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate that LENS outperforms strong baselines, suggesting the practical value of this confidence-based weighting approach. Furthermore, the computational efficiency of the method, as it avoids retraining the base models and introduces minimal overhead, adds to its significance. The generalizability across question-answering tasks also strengthens its claim as a useful ensembling technique. However, the significance remains somewhat bounded by the scope of the tasks considered. More diverse downstream tasks (e.g., summarization, translation) would bolster the paper's broad applicability.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear and well-written paper.
    * Novel approach to confidence estimation for LLM ensembling.
    * Demonstrated improvements over strong baselines.
    * Computationally efficient and doesn't require retraining.
    * Generalizable across different question-answering tasks.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited to decoder-only transformer architectures. The method's applicability to other architectures (e.g., encoder-decoder) needs further investigation.
    * Reliance on access to intermediate layer representations.  This might not be feasible in all production environments or with commercial APIs.
    * The exploration of alternative methods beyond maximum-based selection and sophisticated confidence predictor architectures is limited. A more comprehensive exploration could further enhance system capabilities.
    * The experimental evaluation, while strong, is limited in scope. Expanding to a wider variety of tasks, including generative tasks, would significantly increase the paper's impact.

* **Impact:** The paper provides a valuable contribution to the field of LLM ensembling. LENS offers a practical and efficient method for improving the performance of ensemble systems by leveraging internal representations of LLMs. The code and method being open-source will likely encourage further research.  However, the limitations mentioned above constrain its immediate impact. It's unlikely to revolutionize the field but offers a solid improvement over existing techniques and inspires further exploration of internal representations for ensemble construction.  The paper's impact hinges on whether the method can be extended to other tasks and architectures and deployed in real-world applications.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and effective method for LLM ensembling. The experimental results are solid, and the approach is computationally efficient. However, the limitations regarding architectural constraints and access to internal representations prevent a higher score. Expanding the scope of the evaluation and addressing the limitations would significantly increase its impact and warrant a higher score. The "7" reflects the worthwhile contribution but acknowledges the need for further research to fully realize its potential.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Accessibility Scout: Personalized Accessibility Scans of Built Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23190v1)
- **Authors**: William Huang, Xia Su, Jon E. Froehlich, Yang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Assessing the accessibility of unfamiliar built environments is critical for people with disabilities. However, manual assessments, performed by users or their personal health professionals, are laborious and unscalable, while automatic machine learning methods often neglect an individual user's unique needs. Recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) enable novel approaches to this problem, balancing personalization with scalability to enable more adaptive and context-aware assessments of accessibility. We present Accessibility Scout, an LLM-based accessibility scanning system that identifies accessibility concerns from photos of built environments. With use, Accessibility Scout becomes an increasingly capable "accessibility scout", tailoring accessibility scans to an individual's mobility level, preferences, and specific environmental interests through collaborative Human-AI assessments. We present findings from three studies: a formative study with six participants to inform the design of Accessibility Scout, a technical evaluation of 500 images of built environments, and a user study with 10 participants of varying mobility. Results from our technical evaluation and user study show that Accessibility Scout can generate personalized accessibility scans that extend beyond traditional ADA considerations. Finally, we conclude with a discussion on the implications of our work and future steps for building more scalable and personalized accessibility assessments of the physical world.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Accessibility Scout, an LLM-based system designed to personalize accessibility scans of built environments. The core idea is to move beyond generic accessibility checklists by creating user models that capture individual mobility levels, preferences, and specific environmental interests. These user models, built through collaborative human-AI annotations, are then used to generate tailored accessibility scans from images of various environments (sourced from Yelp, Google Maps, etc.). The system identifies potential tasks and their corresponding accessibility concerns. The paper presents a formative study to inform the design, a technical evaluation of 500 images, and a user study with 10 participants to evaluate the system's performance and usability. Results show that Accessibility Scout can generate personalized scans beyond standard ADA considerations.  The authors discuss implications, limitations, and future work for building more scalable and personalized accessibility assessments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its use of LLMs to create personalized accessibility scans of built environments. Applying LLMs to accessibility assessment is itself innovative, given their ability to reason and contextualize information, though earlier work explores the use of LLMs for accessibility in other areas (e.g., user interface usability or navigation guidance). The integration of visual information with user-specific profiles sets it apart from conventional accessibility auditing tools, which often rely on fixed checklists.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial problem: the limitations of standardized accessibility assessments that often fail to account for individual needs and preferences.  By enabling more adaptive and context-aware assessments, the research has the potential to improve the lives of people with disabilities by helping them make more informed decisions about navigating unfamiliar environments. The ability to scale accessibility evaluation through publicly available images is also significant. The mixed feedback regarding personalized accessibility scans suggests this work addresses a vital need in providing personalized accessibility information.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The study uses a user-centered design approach with a formative study that helps ground the LLM-based accessibility assessment system.
    *   The paper provides a strong technical evaluation using 500 images, and user study with 10 participants (with varying levels of mobility), providing both quantitative and qualitative insights.
    *   The paper leverages the advantages of LLMs by enabling the system to scale and provide more nuanced, context-aware assessments of accessibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The user study has a relatively small sample size. More users would improve the generalizability of the study, but, given that the study is qualitative in nature, the results are well-supported.
    *   The evaluation relies on images, which can be limited compared to dynamic assessment in the real world, or within virtual tours.
    *   The paper acknowledges that reliance on images does not capture dynamic factors.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more direct comparison to existing automated accessibility assessment tools, especially regarding precision and recall in identifying accessibility issues (though the authors correctly note the subjective nature of labeling "correct" accessibility).
    *   The study acknowledges that using a generic LLM can still produce hallucinated results, which needs to be addressed.

*   **Potential Influence:**  Accessibility Scout's framework could influence future research in several ways:

    *   It paves the way for more sophisticated and personalized accessibility assessments that move beyond simple checklists.
    *   It demonstrates the potential for LLMs to be used for visual affordance predictions and environmental understanding in HCI.
    *   It highlights the importance of human-AI collaboration in building and validating accessibility models.
    *   It could inspire new approaches to building adaptive and personalized assistive technologies.

*   **Overall:** While the reliance on images and limited sample size introduce some limitations, the paper presents a significant contribution to the field by introducing a novel system for personalized accessibility assessments. The system's potential to scale and adapt to individual needs makes it a promising step forward in making the built environment more accessible to people with disabilities.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel application of LLMs to a significant real-world problem. Its contributions are practical and scalable, and it demonstrates the potential for LLMs to improve the accuracy and relevance of accessibility assessments. However, the limitations related to image-based assessment, limited sample size, and the need for a more direct comparison to existing tools temper the overall score. Further research to address these limitations would likely enhance the paper's impact and significance.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adversarial-Guided Diffusion for Multimodal LLM Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23202v1)
- **Authors**: Chengwei Xia, Fan Ma, Ruijie Quan, Kun Zhan, Yi Yang
- **Abstract**: This paper addresses the challenge of generating adversarial image using a diffusion model to deceive multimodal large language models (MLLMs) into generating the targeted responses, while avoiding significant distortion of the clean image. To address the above challenges, we propose an adversarial-guided diffusion (AGD) approach for adversarial attack MLLMs. We introduce adversarial-guided noise to ensure attack efficacy. A key observation in our design is that, unlike most traditional adversarial attacks which embed high-frequency perturbations directly into the clean image, AGD injects target semantics into the noise component of the reverse diffusion. Since the added noise in a diffusion model spans the entire frequency spectrum, the adversarial signal embedded within it also inherits this full-spectrum property. Importantly, during reverse diffusion, the adversarial image is formed as a linear combination of the clean image and the noise. Thus, when applying defenses such as a simple low-pass filtering, which act independently on each component, the adversarial image within the noise component is less likely to be suppressed, as it is not confined to the high-frequency band. This makes AGD inherently robust to variety defenses. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our AGD outperforms state-of-the-art methods in attack performance as well as in model robustness to some defenses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Adversarial-Guided Diffusion (AGD), a novel approach for generating adversarial images designed to mislead Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Unlike traditional methods that directly inject high-frequency perturbations into the clean image, AGD strategically embeds target semantics into the noise component during the reverse diffusion process of a diffusion model. This approach leverages the full-spectrum property of noise in diffusion models, making the adversarial signal less susceptible to common defenses like low-pass filtering. The paper demonstrates that AGD achieves superior attack performance and robustness against defenses compared to state-of-the-art methods. It also focuses on preserving the visual quality of the adversarial image to avoid triggering obvious alarms. AGD uses a momentum-based gradient update to guide the diffusion process toward the target, ensuring both attack efficacy and visual imperceptibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach. The idea of injecting target semantics into the *noise* component of the diffusion process, rather than directly manipulating the image pixels, is a key differentiator. This enables a more subtle adversarial attack that is harder to detect and defend against using simple filtering techniques. While diffusion models have been used in adversarial attacks before, the specific strategy of noise-based injection guided by momentum is a novel contribution. The emphasis on defense robustness is also a distinguishing factor.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of the paper stems from addressing a critical vulnerability in MLLMs: their susceptibility to adversarial attacks. By proposing a method that achieves a better trade-off between attack success rate, image quality, and defense robustness, the research contributes to the development of more secure MLLMs.  The results presented show that AGD consistently outperforms SOTA methods in attack performance and robustness, highlighting its practical importance. The analysis of frequency-band properties and the observation that target semantics can be efficiently embedded within the diffusion noise are valuable insights.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of generating effective and robust adversarial attacks against MLLMs.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The AGD method introduces a novel strategy for adversarial image generation using diffusion models, which is distinct from existing approaches.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The extensive experiments demonstrate that AGD outperforms state-of-the-art methods in terms of attack success rate, image quality, and robustness against various defenses across several popular MLLMs.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of the frequency characteristics of the adversarial signal and its implications for defense robustness is insightful.
    *   **Well-written and organized:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making it easy to understand the proposed method and its benefits.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** While not explicitly discussed, diffusion models can be computationally intensive. The added guidance within the diffusion process may further increase computation cost, which could limit its practical applicability.
    *   **Limited Defense Evaluation:**  While the paper tests against several defenses, there's still room for improvement in evaluating against adaptive attacks where the defense mechanism is aware of the attack strategy.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of AGD is likely sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. Although the paper conducts an ablation study, the process of selecting optimal hyperparameter values may be complex and model-specific.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's focus on generating imperceptible and robust adversarial examples for MLLMs could drive further research in this area.  The insights on noise-based embedding of target semantics could be valuable for developing new adversarial attack and defense strategies. The approach may also influence development of more robust and secure MLLMs by guiding model design.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a novel approach, exhibits significant performance gains over existing methods, and provides insightful analysis. While diffusion models have seen prior use in adversarial attacks, the specific approach of injecting target semantics into the noise *before* image construction, combined with the momentum-based guidance is a valuable contribution. This has clear benefits in terms of visual imperceptibility and robustness. However, the potential for high computational cost and limited evaluation of adaptive defenses prevents a higher score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Failures Are the Stepping Stones to Success: Enhancing Few-Shot In-Context Learning by Leveraging Negative Samples
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23211v1)
- **Authors**: Yunhao Liang, Ruixuan Ying, Takuya Taniguchi, Zhe Cui
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models exhibit powerful few-shot in-context learning (ICL) capabilities, but the performance is highly sensitive to provided examples. Recent research has focused on retrieving corresponding examples for each input query, not only enhancing the efficiency and scalability of the learning process but also mitigating inherent biases in manual example selection. However, these studies have primarily emphasized leveraging Positive samples while overlooking the additional information within Negative samples for contextual learning. We propose a novel method that utilizes Negative samples to better select Positive sample examples, thereby enhancing the performance of few-shot ICL. Initially, we construct Positive and Negative sample corpora based on Zero-Shot-Cot. Then, during inference, we employ a semantic similarity-based approach to select the most similar examples from both the Positive and Negative corpora for a given query. Subsequently, we further retrieve Positive examples from the Positive sample corpus based on semantic similarity to the Negative examples, then concatenating them with the previously selected Positive examples to serve as ICL demonstrations. Experimental results demonstrate that our approach surpasses methods solely relying on the most similar positive examples for context, validating that the additional information in negative samples aids in enhancing ICL performance through improved Positive sample selection.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Failures Are the Stepping Stones to Success: Enhancing Few-Shot In-Context Learning by Leveraging Negative Samples".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method to improve the performance of few-shot in-context learning (ICL) in large language models (LLMs) by leveraging negative samples.  Instead of solely relying on positive examples for constructing ICL demonstrations, the authors propose a two-stage framework. First, they construct positive and negative sample corpora by performing zero-shot inference with chain-of-thought prompting and categorize samples based on whether the model correctly answers them. Second, when constructing demonstrations for a test query, they retrieve similar positive and negative examples from their respective corpora. Crucially, they then use the retrieved negative examples to find further, more relevant positive examples.  The intuition is that negative samples highlight areas where the model struggles, and finding positive examples that are semantically similar to those negative samples helps the model learn from its potential errors.  They evaluate their approach across several datasets covering arithmetic, common sense, and symbolic reasoning tasks and demonstrate that their method outperforms baselines that rely solely on positive examples.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of leveraging negative samples to enhance few-shot ICL is novel. While prior work has focused on retrieving and selecting positive examples, actively incorporating negative examples as a means to refine positive example selection represents a new perspective. The two-stage retrieval process, where negative samples are used as a filter to identify additional relevant positive examples, is also a novel contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the robustness and accuracy of ICL. Because ICL is sensitive to the choice of examples, any method that can improve example selection has practical value. By incorporating negative samples, the method attempts to make the demonstration set more informative, potentially mitigating biases and improving generalization. Showing improvements across diverse reasoning tasks strengthens the significance.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the motivation behind leveraging negative samples, drawing an analogy to human learning where correcting errors is crucial.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** The proposed method is clearly defined and relatively straightforward to implement. The two-stage retrieval process is explained well.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides empirical evidence of the effectiveness of their method across several datasets and reasoning tasks. The comparison with several reasonable baselines provides a strong basis for evaluating the proposed approach.
    *   **Quantitative Analysis:** The analysis of the optimal number of negative samples to include demonstrates a thorough experimental approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on CoT and Zero-Shot Performance:** The initial construction of the positive and negative corpora depends on the quality of zero-shot chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting. If the initial zero-shot CoT performance is poor, the resulting corpora may be noisy and inaccurate. The authors do not explicitly address this potential limitation or explore methods to mitigate it.
    *   **GPT-3.5 Turbo:**  The models that have been out since the publishing of the paper have potentially altered the performance of this paper.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Negative Sample Characteristics:** While the paper leverages negative samples, it doesn't delve deeply into the specific characteristics of these samples that make them useful for positive example selection. A deeper analysis of the types of errors the model makes and how the selected positive examples address those errors could provide valuable insights.
    *   **GPT 3.5 Turbo is a large LLM:** Many practitioners don't have the resources to use such a large LLM, and implementing this code on smaller models would be necessary.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in ICL by encouraging researchers to consider the role of negative samples. The proposed method provides a concrete example of how negative samples can be effectively leveraged, and the insights gained from this work could inspire new approaches to prompt engineering and example selection. The concept could also be extended to other few-shot learning settings.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Given the novelty of the approach, the significance of the potential benefits, the clear methodology, and the solid empirical validation, but also considering the limitations related to the dependence on initial zero-shot CoT performance and potential for more in-depth analysis of negative sample characteristics, I would assign a score of **7.5**. The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by introducing a novel idea and demonstrating its effectiveness, but there is still room for further research to address the identified limitations and explore the full potential of leveraging negative samples in ICL.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Zero-Shot Document Understanding using Pseudo Table of Contents-Guided Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23217v1)
- **Authors**: Hyeon Seong Jeong, Sangwoo Jo, Byeong Hyun Yoon, Yoonseok Heo, Haedong Jeong, Taehoon Kim
- **Abstract**: Understanding complex multimodal documents remains challenging due to their structural inconsistencies and limited training data availability. We introduce \textit{DocsRay}, a training-free document understanding system that integrates pseudo Table of Contents (TOC) generation with hierarchical Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). Our approach leverages multimodal Large Language Models' (LLMs) native capabilities to seamlessly process documents containing diverse elements such as text, images, charts, and tables without requiring specialized models or additional training. DocsRay's framework synergistically combines three key techniques: (1) a semantic structuring module using prompt-based LLM interactions to generate a hierarchical pseudo-TOC, (2) zero-shot multimodal analysis that converts diverse document elements into unified, text-centric representations using the inherent capabilities of multimodal LLMs, and (3) an efficient two-stage hierarchical retrieval system that reduces retrieval complexity from $O(N)$ to $O(S + k_1 \cdot N_s)$. Evaluated on documents averaging 49.4 pages and 20,971 textual tokens, DocsRay reduced query latency from 3.89 to 2.12 seconds, achieving a 45% efficiency improvement. On the MMLongBench-Doc benchmark, DocsRay-Pro attains an accuracy of 64.7%, substantially surpassing previous state-of-the-art results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Zero-Shot Document Understanding using Pseudo Table of Contents-Guided Retrieval-Augmented Generation" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DocsRay, a training-free document understanding system that leverages a pseudo Table of Contents (TOC) generated by a multimodal Large Language Model (LLM) combined with hierarchical Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG).  The system aims to address the challenges of understanding complex, unstructured multimodal documents (text, images, tables, etc.) without requiring task-specific training.  DocsRay employs three key components: semantic structure generation using prompts to generate pseudo-TOCs, zero-shot multimodal analysis by converting all document elements into text-centric representations, and a two-stage hierarchical retrieval process to improve efficiency.  The authors demonstrate improved accuracy and efficiency compared to existing methods on the MMLongBench-Doc benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-Free Approach:** The most significant strength is the elimination of task-specific training. This is crucial for real-world deployment where data scarcity and document diversity are common challenges. The ability to deploy DocsRay "out-of-the-box" is a major advantage.
    *   **Effective Integration:** The synergistic integration of pseudo-TOC generation, multimodal processing, and hierarchical retrieval is well-designed.  The authors successfully combine existing techniques in a novel way to address a complex problem. The prompt-based pseudo-TOC generation, in particular, seems to be a cleverly engineered solution for structuring unstructured documents.
    *   **Strong Performance:** The empirical results on MMLongBench-Doc are compelling.  The achieved accuracy of 64.7% substantially outperforms existing baselines, even approaching human-level performance.
    *   **Efficiency Gains:** The hierarchical retrieval method demonstrably reduces computational complexity and query latency, making the system more practical for large documents.
    *   **Comprehensive Ablation and Case Studies:** The paper includes ablation studies that isolate the contributions of the pseudo-TOC and dual embeddings, along with detailed qualitative case studies providing insights into model behaviors and failure modes.
    *   **Explicit discussion of Limitations:** the document mentions existing limitations and the impact of various methods and approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on LLM Capabilities:** The system's performance is inherently tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLM. The quality of the pseudo-TOC relies heavily on the LLM's semantic understanding and prompt engineering.  This means the system's effectiveness may vary depending on the chosen LLM and require adjustments for different models.
    *   **Limited Scope in Multimodal Understanding:** The text-centric representation approach prioritizes semantic retrieval but might sacrifice finer-grained visual understanding, particularly when complex layout analysis or multi-image comparisons are crucial.
    *   **Lack of Semantic Retrieval Metrics:** The absence of dedicated semantic retrieval benchmarks limits a quantitative assessment of the core contribution.  The paper relies heavily on end-to-end QA accuracy, which indirectly reflects retrieval performance.
    *   **Limited Multilingual Evaluation:** The focus on English documents limits the generalizability claims, particularly given that the core architecture's strength is on structuring documents through understanding of semantic content.
    *   **Scope Limitation:** The paper does have limitations in processing specific document types such as images, and requires external data for analysis.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The *integration* of pseudo-TOC generation with hierarchical RAG for zero-shot document understanding is novel.  While the individual components are known, their combination and adaptation for this specific task are original.
    *   The *training-free* nature of the approach has significant practical value, enabling immediate application to diverse document types.
    *   The *semantic* structuring method, relying on LLM-based prompts rather than traditional formatting cues, is a valuable contribution. This provides the ability to understand unstructured, and non formatted documents.
    *   It advances the field by pushing the boundaries of zero-shot document understanding and demonstrating the power of leveraging LLMs for complex information processing tasks.
    *   The discussion and analysis of model scaling, error types, and the effectiveness of the pipeline overall provides valuable insights for future development in this area.
    *   **Explicit discussion of Limitations:** the document mentions existing limitations and the impact of various methods and approaches.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper is likely to influence future research on document understanding, particularly in the direction of training-free approaches and leveraging LLMs for semantic structuring.
    *   The DocsRay system could be adopted in various applications, such as enterprise search, knowledge management, and legal document analysis.
    *   It is likely to encourage the development of more robust semantic retrieval benchmarks for document understanding.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign this paper a score of **8**.

*   The novelty lies in the clever integration of known techniques in a novel and practically useful way. The zero-shot nature of the approach is a significant breakthrough, opening up many opportunities in situations with limited data. The strong empirical results and comprehensive evaluation support the claims.

*   However, the dependency on LLMs, limited multimodal support, and scope limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the approach is a major step forward, the lack of direct evaluation of retrieval quality and limited support of a wider array of languages hold back it's significance and potential impact.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Enabling Few-Shot Alzheimer's Disease Diagnosis on Tabular Biomarker Data with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23227v1)
- **Authors**: Sophie Kearney, Shu Yang, Zixuan Wen, Bojian Hou, Duy Duong-Tran, Tianlong Chen, Jason Moore, Marylyn Ritchie, Li Shen
- **Abstract**: Early and accurate diagnosis of Alzheimer's disease (AD), a complex neurodegenerative disorder, requires analysis of heterogeneous biomarkers (e.g., neuroimaging, genetic risk factors, cognitive tests, and cerebrospinal fluid proteins) typically represented in a tabular format. With flexible few-shot reasoning, multimodal integration, and natural-language-based interpretability, large language models (LLMs) offer unprecedented opportunities for prediction with structured biomedical data. We propose a novel framework called TAP-GPT, Tabular Alzheimer's Prediction GPT, that adapts TableGPT2, a multimodal tabular-specialized LLM originally developed for business intelligence tasks, for AD diagnosis using structured biomarker data with small sample sizes. Our approach constructs few-shot tabular prompts using in-context learning examples from structured biomedical data and finetunes TableGPT2 using the parameter-efficient qLoRA adaption for a clinical binary classification task of AD or cognitively normal (CN). The TAP-GPT framework harnesses the powerful tabular understanding ability of TableGPT2 and the encoded prior knowledge of LLMs to outperform more advanced general-purpose LLMs and a tabular foundation model (TFM) developed for prediction tasks. To our knowledge, this is the first application of LLMs to the prediction task using tabular biomarker data, paving the way for future LLM-driven multi-agent frameworks in biomedical informatics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TAP-GPT, a novel framework for diagnosing Alzheimer's disease (AD) using tabular biomarker data and Large Language Models (LLMs). TAP-GPT adapts TableGPT2, a multimodal LLM designed for tabular data, to classify individuals as having AD or being cognitively normal (CN) based on structured biomarker information (e.g., neuroimaging, genetics, cognitive tests, fluid biomarkers).  The approach utilizes few-shot in-context learning with tabular prompts and parameter-efficient fine-tuning (qLoRA) to adapt the model to the AD classification task.  The results demonstrate that TAP-GPT achieves promising performance, outperforming generic LLMs and a tabular foundation model (TabPFN).  The paper also explores the interpretability of TAP-GPT by generating natural language rationales for its predictions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in its **application of LLMs to the task of AD diagnosis using *tabular* biomarker data**. While LLMs have been used in healthcare and AD research before, this specific combination of tabular data and LLMs, coupled with the few-shot learning approach and use of a tabular-specialized LLM (TableGPT2), is a relatively new contribution. The adaptation of TableGPT2, designed for business intelligence, to a biomedical task is also a notable aspect.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance comes from several factors:
    *   **Data Scarcity:** It addresses the challenge of limited data in biomedical applications. The few-shot learning approach is valuable in situations where large datasets are not available.
    *   **Heterogeneous Biomarkers:**  It offers a method for integrating diverse biomarker data, which is crucial for complex diseases like AD.
    *   **Interpretability:** The inclusion of natural language reasoning capabilities is a key advantage in clinical settings, where understanding the basis for a diagnosis is crucial.  The interpretability analysis, while preliminary, showcases a potentially valuable capability.
    *   **Opening New Avenues:** It pioneers the use of LLMs in this specific application, potentially paving the way for more LLM-driven frameworks in biomedical informatics, multi-agent systems for diagnosis, and more trustworthy AI in healthcare.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-described methodology, including the TAP-GPT framework, data splitting strategy, and prompt engineering.
    *   Demonstration of promising empirical results, outperforming baselines.
    *   Exploration of interpretability through CoT reasoning.
    *   Thorough ablation studies to analyze the impact of different components.
    *   Releasing code and data for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dataset used, while standard, has a relatively small sample size (333 patients). This limits the generalizability of the results.
    *   The interpretability analysis is limited to a few examples.  A more systematic evaluation of the quality and accuracy of the generated rationales would strengthen the paper.
    *   The paper states they are only finetuning the LLM decoder in TableGPT2 but should mention that the architecture used is not publicly available and they are limited by the scope of TableGPT2's LLM decoder.
    *   The study focuses on binary classification (AD vs. CN).  Expanding to more complex diagnostic categories (e.g., MCI) could be a valuable extension.
    *   Reliance on TabPFN as a benchmark could be problematic because it has a high memory footprint and its performance is not consistently state of the art in every scenario.
    *   There should be discussion on TableGPT2, a multimodal LLM for tabular data, as being a black box model that is hard to change.

*   **Justification for the Score:** While the paper addresses an important problem with a novel approach and demonstrates promising results, some limitations prevent it from receiving a higher score. The limited dataset size, the preliminary nature of the interpretability analysis, the black box nature of TableGPT2, and the heavy reliance on TabPFN as a foundation model constrain the study's impact. However, the positive aspects are that it does achieve good performance, with code available, while introducing a new domain to study LLMs.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fine-Grained Privacy Extraction from Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems via Knowledge Asymmetry Exploitation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23229v1)
- **Authors**: Yufei Chen, Yao Wang, Haibin Zhang, Tao Gu
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems enhance large language models (LLMs) by integrating external knowledge bases, but this advancement introduces significant privacy risks. Existing privacy attacks on RAG systems can trigger data leakage but often fail to accurately isolate knowledge-base-derived sentences within mixed responses. They also lack robustness when applied across multiple domains. This paper addresses these challenges by presenting a novel black-box attack framework that exploits knowledge asymmetry between RAG and standard LLMs to achieve fine-grained privacy extraction across heterogeneous knowledge landscapes. We propose a chain-of-thought reasoning strategy that creates adaptive prompts to steer RAG systems away from sensitive content. Specifically, we first decompose adversarial queries to maximize information disparity and then apply a semantic relationship scoring to resolve lexical and syntactic ambiguities. We finally train a neural network on these feature scores to precisely identify sentences containing private information. Unlike prior work, our framework generalizes to unseen domains through iterative refinement without pre-defined knowledge. Experimental results show that we achieve over 91% privacy extraction rate in single-domain and 83% in multi-domain scenarios, reducing sensitive sentence exposure by over 65% in case studies. This work bridges the gap between attack and defense in RAG systems, enabling precise extraction of private information while providing a foundation for adaptive mitigation.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Fine-Grained Privacy Extraction from Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems via Knowledge Asymmetry Exploitation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the privacy risks associated with Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. RAG systems enhance Large Language Models (LLMs) by integrating external knowledge bases, but this can inadvertently expose private information contained in those knowledge bases.  The authors propose a novel black-box attack framework that exploits the *knowledge asymmetry* between a RAG system and a standard LLM to achieve fine-grained privacy extraction.  Their approach uses a chain-of-thought reasoning strategy to create adaptive prompts that steer RAG systems toward revealing sensitive content, decomposing queries to maximize information disparity, applying semantic relationship scoring to resolve ambiguities, and finally, using a neural network to precisely identify sentences containing private information. The framework generalizes to unseen domains through iterative query refinement without requiring pre-defined knowledge.  Experimental results demonstrate a high privacy extraction rate in both single-domain and multi-domain scenarios.  The authors also propose a defense mechanism based on their attack findings, using chain-of-thought prompts to generate privacy-preserving responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a few aspects of novelty:

    *   **Fine-grained Localization:**  Existing attacks typically only detect the presence of private data without identifying the specific sentences that are leaking the information. This paper directly addresses this by attempting to pinpoint the exact sentences in the RAG response derived from the knowledge base. This sentence-level attribution is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Knowledge Asymmetry Exploitation:** The key idea of leveraging the difference in knowledge between RAG systems and standard LLMs is a clever approach. This allows the attack to operate in a black-box setting, requiring only access to the RAG system's input and output.
    *   **Iterative Query Refinement for Multi-Domain Generalization:**  The ability to adapt to new domains without pre-defined knowledge is a significant advantage. Most previous privacy attacks are tailored to specific domains or rely on domain-specific knowledge or manual tuning, limiting their real-world applicability. The iterative refinement allows the attack to function in more open-ended settings.
    *   **Attack and Defense Pipeline:** By presenting both an attack and defense mechanism the work is strengthened.  The ability to use the attack findings to guide the generation of privacy-preserving responses is a compelling feature.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Addressing a Critical Problem:**  As RAG systems become more widely adopted in sensitive domains (healthcare, finance, law), the privacy risks they pose become increasingly important. This paper directly addresses a critical security concern.
    *   **Practical Relevance:**  The black-box nature of the attack makes it highly relevant to real-world deployments where attackers typically lack access to internal system components. The multi-domain generalization also increases its practical value.
    *   **Potential for Impact:**  The proposed techniques could inform the design of more robust RAG systems and contribute to the development of effective privacy-preserving mechanisms. The insights gained from analyzing knowledge asymmetry can be beneficial in designing mitigation strategies.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The paper includes comprehensive experimental evaluations on diverse datasets and RAG configurations, strengthening the validity of the findings.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly written and well-structured paper.
    *   The problem is well-motivated and the proposed solution is technically sound.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with ablation studies.
    *   The combined attack and defense approach is a strong point.
    *   Addresses the limitation of prior methods (single domain and lack of sentence-level localization).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the multi-domain generalization is a strength, the ESR (Extraction Success Rate) still drops from 91% in single-domain scenarios to 83% in multi-domain scenarios. This suggests there's room for further improvement in the generalization capabilities.
    *   The complexity of the iterative query refinement process and the computational resources required could be a barrier to widespread adoption and replication.  The authors acknowledge the "time cost" in the limitations section.  Further optimizations or simplifications could enhance its practicality.
    * The reliance on a large labeled dataset for classifier training is a bottleneck. While the iterative refinement allows the models to apply to new domains, the classifier still needs initial training data. This can reduce the generalizability to more novel situations, and introduces a reliance on human labels.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:

    *   Privacy-preserving RAG systems
    *   Black-box attack strategies
    *   Knowledge base security
    *   Adversarial prompt engineering.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper offers a significant advancement in the area of privacy attacks against RAG systems. The fine-grained localization of private data, the black-box nature of the attack, and the ability to generalize across multiple domains are all important contributions. The proposed defense mechanism further enhances the paper's value. While the drop in ESR in multi-domain scenarios and the computational cost are limitations, the paper's overall impact on the field is substantial.

Score: 8

**Rigorous Rationale:** The paper significantly advances the state-of-the-art in privacy extraction from RAG systems by addressing the key limitations of existing methods. The work exhibits strong novelty and practical relevance, as evidenced by the comprehensive experimental evaluation and the inclusion of both an attack and a defense strategy. The limitations relating to computation costs prevent the work from being given a score in the top echelon, but it undoubtedly constitutes a very strong contribution that will stimulate further research.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### P-ReMIS: Pragmatic Reasoning in Mental Health and a Social Implication
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23247v1)
- **Authors**: Sneha Oram, Pushpak Bhattacharyya
- **Abstract**: There has been an increase in recent advancements in the explainability and development of personalized chatbots for mental health. However, the reasoning aspects for explainability and dialogue discourse have not been explored previously for mental health. Hence, we are investigating the pragmatic reasoning capability of large language models (LLMs) in this domain. We introduce P-ReMe dataset, and propose a modified definition for the pragmatic phenomena of implicature (implied meaning) and presupposition (implicit assumption) in mental health. Following the definition, we formulate two tasks in implicature and one task in presupposition. To benchmark the dataset and the presented tasks, we consider four models - Llama3.1, Mistral, MentaLLaMa, and Qwen. The results of the experiments suggest that Mistral and Qwen show substantial reasoning capabilities in the domain. In addition, we also propose StiPRompts to study the stigma around mental health with the state-of-the-art LLMs, GPT-4o mini, Deepseek-chat, and Claude-3.5-haiku. Our evaluated findings show that Claude-3.5-haiku deals with the stigma more responsibly compared to the other two LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces P-ReMIS, a dataset and evaluation framework focused on pragmatic reasoning in the context of mental health. The authors argue that existing work often overlooks the nuances of implicature and presupposition in this domain, particularly regarding emotional valence and negative sentiment. They propose modified definitions of these pragmatic phenomena tailored for mental health contexts and formulate corresponding tasks: agreement detection, implicature natural language inference (NLI), and presupposition NLI. The dataset, P-ReMe, is constructed by combining real social media data from Reddit (CAMS dataset) with synthetically generated data using GPT-40 mini. They benchmark several instruction-tuned LLMs (LlaMa3.1, Mistral, MentaLLaMa, Qwen) on these tasks.  Furthermore, the paper proposes StiPRompts, a set of adversarial prompts designed to assess how LLMs handle stigmatizing statements related to mental health, evaluating the responses of GPT-40 mini, Claude-3.5 haiku, and Deepseek-chat.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its focused application of pragmatic reasoning concepts to the mental health domain. While implicature and presupposition are well-established in NLP, the adaptation and redefinition of these concepts to account for the specific characteristics of mental health language (e.g., high negative sentiment, low emotional valence) is a valuable contribution. The construction of a new dataset, P-ReMe, specifically designed for these tasks also adds to the paper's novelty, even though it builds upon an existing dataset. The idea of using adversarial "StiPRompts" to probe the handling of stigma by LLMs is also a creative approach.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from the increasing use of LLMs in mental health applications, such as empathetic chatbots and diagnostic tools.  Improving the pragmatic reasoning abilities of these models can lead to more accurate and sensitive interactions, potentially benefiting both patients and mental health professionals. Evaluating how LLMs handle stigmatizing content is crucial from an ethical and social responsibility perspective.  The benchmark results provide insights into the current capabilities of different LLMs in this domain. The limitations are acknowledged by the authors.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly articulates the gap in existing research and motivates the need for specialized pragmatic reasoning in mental health.
