### What's Taboo for You? - An Empirical Evaluation of LLMs Behavior Toward Sensitive Content
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23319v1)
- **Authors**: Alfio Ferrara, Sergio Picascia, Laura Pinnavaia, Vojimir Ranitovic, Elisabetta Rocchetti, Alice Tuveri
- **Abstract**: Proprietary Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown tendencies toward politeness, formality, and implicit content moderation. While previous research has primarily focused on explicitly training models to moderate and detoxify sensitive content, there has been limited exploration of whether LLMs implicitly sanitize language without explicit instructions. This study empirically analyzes the implicit moderation behavior of GPT-4o-mini when paraphrasing sensitive content and evaluates the extent of sensitivity shifts. Our experiments indicate that GPT-4o-mini systematically moderates content toward less sensitive classes, with substantial reductions in derogatory and taboo language. Also, we evaluate the zero-shot capabilities of LLMs in classifying sentence sensitivity, comparing their performances against traditional methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the implicit content moderation behavior of proprietary large language models (LLMs) when paraphrasing sensitive content. Specifically, it focuses on GPT-40-mini and assesses whether, without explicit instructions, the model sanitizes language, shifting it towards less sensitive categories. The study involves creating a dataset of sensitive expressions, collecting sentences containing them, and generating paraphrases using GPT-40-mini. Human experts and automated classifiers (open-source LLMs and traditional methods) then annotate these sentences based on a defined sensitivity scale (Formal/Polite, Informal, Derogatory, Taboo). The study then compares original and paraphrased sentences to quantify shifts in sensitivity, and to assess the effectiveness of the automated classifiers in replicating human judgments. The results suggest that GPT-40-mini does indeed implicitly moderate content, particularly reducing derogatory and taboo language.  The paper also evaluates open-source LLMs and traditional classifiers for their alignment with expert annotations in sensitivity detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of evaluating implicit content moderation by LLMs is a relevant and timely research direction. While prior work has focused on explicit detoxification and style transfer, this paper shines light on the potentially unintended content transformation during paraphrasing. To some extent, the paper builds upon existing techniques related to content moderation and detoxification, but by framing the investigation around the *implicit* moderation tendencies, the paper offers a new perspective. It makes a novel contribution by empirically quantifying this behavior, rather than simply observing it anecdotally. The study also adds value by comparing human judgments to automated classifiers including open-source LLMs. The novelty is moderate.

*   **Significance:** Content moderation is a crucial aspect of deploying LLMs responsibly.  Understanding how these models inherently alter sensitive content during general tasks like paraphrasing is important for several reasons. First, it reveals potential biases or limitations. Second, it informs development of more nuanced content moderation systems. Finally, it highlights the subtle ethical considerations involved in using these models. The paper contributes to the broader discussion on fairness, accountability, and transparency in AI, particularly in the context of language models. The significance is considerable for LLMs developers and practitioners.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined research questions:** The research questions are clear and targeted, focusing on implicit moderation and the alignment of automated classifications with human judgment.
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The study employs a systematic approach, from dataset construction and annotation to evaluation metrics. The use of both human experts and automated methods provides a robust analysis. The paper also leverages statistical significance testing to validate observations.
    *   **Relevance:** The findings directly address the growing concerns about content moderation in LLMs and can inform best practices.
    *   **Transparency:** The paper provides detailed information about the dataset creation, annotation process, and evaluation metrics.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Model dependency:** The findings are based on GPT-40-mini, which might not generalize to all proprietary LLMs. Exploring more models is necessary.
    *   **Dataset limitations:** While diverse, the dataset might not cover all forms of sensitive language or reflect the full complexity of real-world content.
    *   **Subjectivity in annotation:** Sensitivity is inherently subjective, and even with detailed guidelines, annotator bias cannot be entirely eliminated. The paper acknowledges this, but further mitigation strategies (e.g., more annotators, inter-annotator agreement analysis) could strengthen the findings.
    *   **Limited exploration of open-source LLMs:** The zero-shot prompting method used with the open-source LLMs might not be optimal. Fine-tuning or alternative prompting strategies could reveal better performance.
    *   **Lack of analysis of the semantic fidelity of paraphrases:** The paper focuses on the sensitivity shift, but it would have been useful to evaluate if the paraphrases maintained semantic similarity with the original sentences. A significant alteration of meaning would make the sensitivity shift results less impactful.
    *   **Scope**: The analysis is somewhat limited to detecting the shifts, but could be extended to analyze the specific types of modifications (word replacements, sentence structure changes) causing the shift.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more responsible LLMs. By highlighting the implicit moderation behavior, it encourages developers to be more aware of unintended consequences. The comparison of automated classifiers can also inform the design of more efficient content moderation systems.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a timely and relevant investigation into an under-explored aspect of LLM behavior. The systematic methodology and empirical findings contribute to the field by quantifying implicit content moderation. However, the limitations related to model dependency, dataset scope, and the absence of a more in-depth analysis of the open-source LLMs prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. Further, while novel, it is building on an established literature.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MUST-RAG: MUSical Text Question Answering with Retrieval Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23334v1)
- **Authors**: Daeyong Kwon, SeungHeon Doh, Juhan Nam
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities across diverse domains. While they exhibit strong zero-shot performance on various tasks, LLMs' effectiveness in music-related applications remains limited due to the relatively small proportion of music-specific knowledge in their training data. To address this limitation, we propose MusT-RAG, a comprehensive framework based on Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) to adapt general-purpose LLMs for text-only music question answering (MQA) tasks. RAG is a technique that provides external knowledge to LLMs by retrieving relevant context information when generating answers to questions. To optimize RAG for the music domain, we (1) propose MusWikiDB, a music-specialized vector database for the retrieval stage, and (2) utilizes context information during both inference and fine-tuning processes to effectively transform general-purpose LLMs into music-specific models. Our experiment demonstrates that MusT-RAG significantly outperforms traditional fine-tuning approaches in enhancing LLMs' music domain adaptation capabilities, showing consistent improvements across both in-domain and out-of-domain MQA benchmarks. Additionally, our MusWikiDB proves substantially more effective than general Wikipedia corpora, delivering superior performance and computational efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MusT-RAG, a framework that uses Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) to improve the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) on text-based music question answering (MQA) tasks.  The framework includes MusWikiDB, a music-specific vector database designed for RAG. The authors augment LLMs with external knowledge retrieval mechanisms by retrieving relevant context from MusWikiDB. They also explore a RAG-style fine-tuning approach that incorporates context during training, which, according to their experiments, outperforms standard fine-tuning. The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of MusT-RAG on both in-domain and out-of-domain MQA benchmarks. The key contributions are the MusT-RAG framework itself, the MusWikiDB database, and the demonstration that RAG-style fine-tuning improves contextual understanding. The paper also introduces a new benchmark, ArtistMus, for evaluating artist-related questions in text-only MQA.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of using RAG for music-related tasks isn't entirely new, as RAG has been applied in various domains before. However, the specific application to MQA in a text-only setting, the creation of a dedicated music-specific vector database (MusWikiDB), and the exploration of RAG-style fine-tuning contribute to the novelty of the paper. The ArtistMus benchmark also fills a gap in existing MQA evaluations.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a practical problem: the limited ability of general-purpose LLMs to handle music-specific questions due to insufficient music knowledge in their training data. By leveraging RAG, the framework allows LLMs to access and utilize external music knowledge, improving their accuracy and contextual understanding. The performance gains demonstrated in the experiments, particularly with RAG fine-tuning, suggest that the proposed approach is effective. MusWikiDB can serve as a valuable resource for future research in music information retrieval and MQA.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Framework:** MusT-RAG offers a well-defined and complete framework for music-related question answering, incorporating both retrieval and generation components.
    * **Music-Specific Database:** The creation of MusWikiDB is a significant contribution, providing a curated resource for RAG in the music domain. The ablation studies show its effectiveness compared to general Wikipedia corpora.
    * **RAG Fine-tuning:** The exploration of RAG-style fine-tuning is insightful and demonstrates the benefits of incorporating context during training. The results show improvements over standard fine-tuning and RAG inference.
    * **Empirical Validation:**  The paper presents thorough experimental results on both in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness and generalizability of MusT-RAG.
    * **Addresses a Real Problem:** Focuses on an important and current limitation of LLMs within the music domain.
    * **Reproducibility:** The paper provides sufficient detail about the methods, datasets, and training configurations, making the work potentially reproducible.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Model Evaluation:** The primary model used is Llama 3.1 8B Instruct.  Exploring other LLMs, especially larger models or models specifically designed for instruction following, could strengthen the findings.  While GPT-4 is used as a zero-shot baseline and to help with data generation, a direct comparison by fine-tuning GPT-4 or another high-performing LLM would be informative.
    * **Retriever Choice:** The ablation study explored different passage sizes and embeddings, but the best retrieval method (BM25 in this case) could be relatively basic.  More advanced retrieval techniques incorporating contextual embeddings might lead to further improvements.
    * **Domain Specificity Limitations:** While MusWikiDB demonstrates a substantial improvement over the generic Wikipedia dataset, its utility might be capped if the questions necessitate more nuanced or uncommon musical knowledge. The reliance on a database potentially prevents dealing with less known music entities.
    * **Dataset Generation:** The use of GPT-4 for dataset generation is helpful but introduces a potential for biases in the generated questions and answers. Human validation mitigates this somewhat, but it's still a consideration.  More diverse question types or the inclusion of negative examples might improve the robustness of the models.

* **Justification for Score:**

MusT-RAG presents a solid and well-executed approach to improving MQA performance in LLMs using RAG. The creation of MusWikiDB addresses a specific need, and the results are compelling. While the methodology isn't radically new (RAG itself is an established technique), the specific application to music, the curated database, and the RAG-style fine-tuning make valuable contributions. The new ArtistMus benchmark is also useful. The weaknesses, such as the limited model evaluation and the use of GPT-4 for dataset creation, are factors that could be addressed in future work. Overall, the paper has a good balance of novelty, significance, and empirical validation.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DSBC : Data Science task Benchmarking with Context engineering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23336v1)
- **Authors**: Ram Mohan Rao Kadiyala, Siddhant Gupta, Jebish Purbey, Giulio Martini, Suman Debnath, Hamza Farooq
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have significantly impacted data science workflows, giving rise to specialized data science agents designed to automate analytical tasks. Despite rapid adoption, systematic benchmarks evaluating the efficacy and limitations of these agents remain scarce. In this paper, we introduce a comprehensive benchmark specifically crafted to reflect real-world user interactions with data science agents by observing usage of our commercial applications. We evaluate three LLMs: Claude-4.0-Sonnet, Gemini-2.5-Flash, and OpenAI-o4-Mini across three approaches: zero-shot with context engineering, multi-step with context engineering, and with SmolAgent. Our benchmark assesses performance across a diverse set of eight data science task categories, additionally exploring the sensitivity of models to common prompting issues, such as data leakage and slightly ambiguous instructions. We further investigate the influence of temperature parameters on overall and task-specific outcomes for each model and approach. Our findings reveal distinct performance disparities among the evaluated models and methodologies, highlighting critical factors that affect practical deployment. The benchmark dataset and evaluation framework introduced herein aim to provide a foundation for future research of more robust and effective data science agents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DSBC paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DSBC, a new benchmark designed to evaluate data science agents.  DSBC aims to address a perceived gap in existing benchmarks by focusing on real-world user interactions, derived from observations of commercial data science agent usage.  It evaluates three large language models (LLMs) - Claude-4.0-Sonnet, Gemini-2.5-Flash, and OpenAI-04-Mini - using three approaches: zero-shot with context engineering, multi-step with context engineering, and with SmolAgent. The benchmark assesses performance across eight data science task categories and explores sensitivity to prompting issues and temperature parameters. The authors provide a structured approach to provide data file description through Context Engineering and provide the models with the required data file details through that.  The paper highlights performance differences among models and methodologies, emphasizing factors crucial for practical deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its emphasis on realistic user interactions and the introduction of "Context Engineering". Many existing benchmarks rely on synthetic data or manually crafted descriptions. The methodology to automatically create context from the data itself is definitely a notable aspect. However, while context engineering is a useful contribution, the overall benchmark structure, task categories and models tested are somewhat derivative of previous work in this space. A more in-depth novel contribution would be to focus on model routing through the generated metadata.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is moderate. Benchmarks are crucial for driving progress in data science agents, and a focus on real-world usage patterns can help align research with practical needs. The benchmark covers the common use cases, which are very much relevant in the recent times. However, the experiments are limited to comparing current LLMs on the proposed dataset; further analysis into the dataset and error categories would improve it's value. This is even more pronounced when considered the dataset's size.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Real-World Focus:** The use of real user interaction data makes the benchmark more relevant.
    *   **Context Engineering:** The automated approach to generating data file descriptions is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study investigates various models, methodologies, and parameters.
    *   **Release of Dataset and Framework:** This promotes reproducibility and future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM versions:** LLM versions are rapidly evolving, and the models used in this benchmark may be outdated soon. This significantly limits the long-term value of the results, although the underlying evaluation framework remains relevant.
    *   **Limited Dataset Diversity:** The dataset is sourced from Kaggle which includes specific types of datasets. A wider selection from diverse sources would improve robustness.
    *   **Overclaim of conclusions:** The claim that Context Engineering accurately reflects user interactions could be strengthened with more detailed validation comparing the generated metadata.
    *   **Small Scale:** Some results aren't statistically significant given the dataset's relative size, which limits its strength.

*   **Potential Influence:** The benchmark could influence future research by encouraging the development of more robust and practical data science agents. However, its limited novelty and the evolving landscape of LLMs may diminish its long-term impact.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a useful but limited contribution to the field of data science agent benchmarking. The emphasis on real-world user interactions and context engineering are positive aspects. However, the study lacks significant novelty, experimental depth, and the results are limited by the nature of rapidly-changing LLMs. Furthermore, the contribution is based on small amount of data. The benchmark is likely to become dated relatively quickly.

Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 5/10

### SWE-Debate: Competitive Multi-Agent Debate for Software Issue Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23348v1)
- **Authors**: Han Li, Yuling Shi, Shaoxin Lin, Xiaodong Gu, Heng Lian, Xin Wang, Yantao Jia, Tao Huang, Qianxiang Wang
- **Abstract**: Issue resolution has made remarkable progress thanks to the advanced reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). Recently, agent-based frameworks such as SWE-agent have further advanced this progress by enabling autonomous, tool-using agents to tackle complex software engineering tasks. While existing agent-based issue resolution approaches are primarily based on agents' independent explorations, they often get stuck in local solutions and fail to identify issue patterns that span across different parts of the codebase. To address this limitation, we propose SWE-Debate, a competitive multi-agent debate framework that encourages diverse reasoning paths and achieves more consolidated issue localization. SWE-Debate first creates multiple fault propagation traces as localization proposals by traversing a code dependency graph. Then, it organizes a three-round debate among specialized agents, each embodying distinct reasoning perspectives along the fault propagation trace. This structured competition enables agents to collaboratively converge on a consolidated fix plan. Finally, this consolidated fix plan is integrated into an MCTS-based code modification agent for patch generation. Experiments on the SWE-bench benchmark show that SWE-Debate achieves new state-of-the-art results in open-source agent frameworks and outperforms baselines by a large margin.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SWE-Debate: Competitive Multi-Agent Debate for Software Issue Resolution":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SWE-Debate, a novel framework designed to improve software issue resolution by employing a competitive multi-agent debate strategy.  It addresses the limitations of existing agent-based approaches, which often get stuck in local solutions due to a limited observation scope. SWE-Debate operates in three stages: 1) generating multiple candidate fault propagation traces using dependency analysis, 2) orchestrating a structured three-round debate among specialized agents representing different reasoning perspectives along the trace, and 3) integrating the consolidated fix plan from the debate into an MCTS-based code modification agent for patch generation.  Experiments on the SWE-bench benchmark demonstrate that SWE-Debate achieves state-of-the-art results compared to existing open-source agent frameworks.  The paper emphasizes the importance of diverse reasoning paths and consolidated issue localization for effective software repair.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the competitive multi-agent debate framework applied to software issue resolution.  While individual components such as dependency graph analysis and MCTS are not entirely new, the combination and structured debate process are innovative.  The idea of forcing agents to defend their positions and critique alternatives provides a mechanism for overcoming limitations of individual exploration, representing a unique contribution. The concept of multiple initial chains/fault propagation paths is also valuable as it enables a more comprehensive search.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial and persistent challenge in software engineering: automated issue resolution. By improving fault localization and code modification accuracy, SWE-Debate has the potential to significantly reduce debugging time and improve software quality.  The experimental results on SWE-bench, a widely recognized benchmark, support the claim of improved performance, further demonstrating the significance of the work. The ablation studies provides solid evidence for the individual contributions of its components to the overall system performance.

* **Strengths:**
    *  The paper clearly articulates the problem of limited observation scope in existing agent-based systems.
    *  The proposed SWE-Debate framework is well-defined and logically structured.
    *  The use of competitive debate is a novel and compelling approach to improving fault localization.
    *  The experimental results on SWE-bench demonstrate significant performance improvements.
    *  Ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
    * The detailed case study allows for a more understandable demonstration of the tool's effectiveness.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *  While the experiments are performed on SWE-bench, the results might not generalize to all types of software projects. The benchmark consists of relatively small, self-contained issues. Real-world repositories often have more complex dependencies and larger codebases, potentially presenting new challenges.
    *  The implementation details section mentions that testbed set up was unsuccessful for some models, limiting its range of evaluations.
    *  The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the computational cost associated with the multi-agent debate process.  The trade-off between accuracy and computational resources needs to be considered, especially for large-scale software projects.
    * The current multi-agent debate relies on a single foundation model to ensure coherence. Future work could explore the usage of heterogeneous language models with varying reasoning skills and focus on orchestrating different foundation models within the framework.

* **Potential Influence:** SWE-Debate has the potential to influence future research in automated software repair by shifting the focus from individual agent exploration to collaborative and competitive reasoning. The framework can serve as a foundation for developing more robust and accurate issue resolution systems.  The concept of incorporating diverse perspectives through structured debate could be applied to other software engineering tasks such as code review, requirements elicitation, and design optimization. The work will likely encourage the exploration of diverse reasoning paths and consolidate issue localization.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** SWE-Debate presents a significant and novel contribution to automated software issue resolution. While there are limitations regarding the generalizability of results and computational cost, the proposed competitive multi-agent debate framework offers a compelling and promising approach. The significant performance improvements demonstrated on SWE-bench and the rigorous ablation studies support the significance of the work. The paper addresses a well-defined problem, presents a novel solution, and provides strong empirical evidence of its effectiveness. The potential for influence in future research directions warrants a high score. Further, the paper presents several research opportunities to further improve the approach that will impact the domain in the future.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### IN45023 Neural Network Design Patterns in Computer Vision Seminar Report, Summer 2025
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23357v1)
- **Authors**: Radu-Andrei Bourceanu, Neil De La Fuente, Jan Grimm, Andrei Jardan, Andriy Manucharyan, Cornelius Weiss, Roman Pflugfelder
- **Abstract**: This report analyzes the evolution of key design patterns in computer vision by examining six influential papers. The analy- sis begins with foundational architectures for image recognition. We review ResNet, which introduced residual connections to overcome the vanishing gradient problem and enable effective training of significantly deeper convolutional networks. Subsequently, we examine the Vision Transformer (ViT), which established a new paradigm by applying the Transformer ar- chitecture to sequences of image patches, demonstrating the efficacy of attention-based models for large-scale image recogni- tion. Building on these visual representation backbones, we investigate generative models. Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) are analyzed for their novel adversarial training process, which challenges a generator against a discriminator to learn complex data distributions. Then, Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs) are covered, which improve upon prior generative methods by performing a sequential denoising process in a perceptually compressed latent space. LDMs achieve high-fidelity synthesis with greater computational efficiency, representing the current state-of-the-art for image generation. Finally, we explore self-supervised learning techniques that reduce dependency on labeled data. DINO is a self-distillation framework in which a student network learns to match the output of a momentum-updated teacher, yielding features with strong k-NN classification performance. We conclude with Masked Autoencoders (MAE), which utilize an asymmetric encoder-decoder design to reconstruct heavily masked inputs, providing a highly scalable and effective method for pre-training large-scale vision models.
- **Summary**: This paper analyzes the evolution of key design patterns in computer vision by examining six influential papers: ResNet, Vision Transformer (ViT), Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs), DINO, and Masked Autoencoders (MAE). The report traces the progression from foundational architectures for image recognition to generative modeling and self-supervised learning, highlighting the contributions of each paper in overcoming challenges and advancing the field. It provides a technical overview of each method, including their background, motivation, approach, methodology, and experimental results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper provides a good overview of several influential papers in computer vision. However, its novelty is limited to summarizing existing research. While it effectively presents the core ideas and contributions of each paper, it lacks a deeper critical analysis and synthesis. The report mainly paraphrases the content of the original papers without providing substantial original insights or uncovering novel connections between them. The analysis of each method is descriptive rather than analytical, missing an opportunity to critically evaluate the limitations, trade-offs, or potential future directions for each approach.

The significance of the paper is moderate. It could be useful for students or researchers seeking a concise introduction to these key topics, but it offers little new information to those already familiar with the literature. It does not contribute to advancing the research frontier. While the paper touches upon self-supervised learning and its importance, it could have analyzed and pointed out the possible pitfalls associated with SSL. Some common pitfalls are: high computational cost, increased dataset sensitivity and biased training.

Justification for the Score:

The score reflects the lack of original contribution and the descriptive nature of the analysis. While the paper is well-organized and covers important topics, it does not offer new insights or a critical perspective that would justify a higher score. The paper successfully explains the key ideas of influential papers, but fails to provide any substantial evaluation or synthesis beyond what is already present in the original works.

Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 5/10

### Text-to-SQL Task-oriented Dialogue Ontology Construction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23358v1)
- **Authors**: Renato Vukovic, Carel van Niekerk, Michael Heck, Benjamin Ruppik, Hsien-Chin Lin, Shutong Feng, Nurul Lubis, Milica Gasic
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are widely used as general-purpose knowledge sources, but they rely on parametric knowledge, limiting explainability and trustworthiness. In task-oriented dialogue (TOD) systems, this separation is explicit, using an external database structured by an explicit ontology to ensure explainability and controllability. However, building such ontologies requires manual labels or supervised training. We introduce TeQoDO: a Text-to-SQL task-oriented Dialogue Ontology construction method. Here, an LLM autonomously builds a TOD ontology from scratch without supervision using its inherent SQL programming capabilities combined with dialogue theory provided in the prompt. We show that TeQoDO outperforms transfer learning approaches, and its constructed ontology is competitive on a downstream dialogue state tracking task. Ablation studies demonstrate the key role of dialogue theory. TeQoDO also scales to allow construction of much larger ontologies, which we investigate on a Wikipedia and ArXiv dataset. We view this as a step towards broader application of ontologies to increase LLM explainability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TeQoDO, a novel Text-to-SQL method for autonomously constructing task-oriented dialogue (TOD) ontologies using Large Language Models (LLMs).  Unlike existing approaches that require manual labeling or supervised training, TeQoDO leverages LLMs' inherent SQL programming capabilities, guided by dialogue theory, to incrementally build ontologies from scratch.  The method iteratively queries, tracks the dialogue state, and updates a database in SQL, effectively extracting domains, slots, and values from raw dialogue data. The paper demonstrates that TeQoDO outperforms transfer learning methods in TOD ontology construction and achieves competitive performance in downstream dialogue state tracking. The method is also shown to scale to larger, general knowledge datasets like Wikipedia and ArXiv. The authors emphasize the role of ontologies in enhancing LLM explainability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using Text-to-SQL generation within LLMs to *construct* TOD ontologies from dialogue data is moderately novel. Prior work has used LLMs for relation extraction and ontology population *given* a pre-existing ontology or schema (or to *populate* one with *existing* ground truth ontology terms).  The "from-scratch" aspect of automatically creating the schema itself based on dialogue interaction is a step forward.  Integrating explicit dialogue theory (dialogue state tracking and a notion of "success") into the SQL generation process to improve ontology construction is a further increment. However, the underlying components (LLMs, Text-to-SQL, dialogue state tracking) are all well-established.
* **Significance:** The potential impact of this work lies in automating and simplifying the creation of TOD ontologies. If effective, it could significantly reduce the manual effort required to build and maintain knowledge bases for dialogue systems. The demonstrated scalability to Wikipedia and ArXiv datasets also suggests broader applicability beyond TOD, potentially contributing to more structured knowledge representation for various LLM-based applications. This approach helps in constructing human-readable ontologies, which directly address the issue of explainability in LLMs and is becoming increasingly important.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Automation:** The key advantage is the autonomous construction of ontologies without explicit supervision.
    *   **Performance:** The paper demonstrates competitive or superior performance compared to supervised methods on TOD tasks and performs reasonably well on general knowledge datasets (Wikipedia, ArXiv).
    *   **Scalability:** The results on larger datasets, though requiring some adjustments, suggest the potential for scaling TeQoDO to even broader domains.
    *   **Explainability:** Linking extracted knowledge to a structured ontology makes LLM behavior more interpretable.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **SQL Limitations:**  SQL, while providing structure, may not be the ideal representation for complex semantic relationships.  The paper acknowledges that SQL struggles with multiple hierarchy levels.
    *   **Prompt Sensitivity:** The authors concede the prompt engineering for the LLM is crucial, which can be a practical limitation, as prompt engineering is still largely considered an art. While they provide prompts in the appendix, sensitivity to prompt phrasing remains a drawback.
    *   **Lack of Error Analysis:** While the quantitative evaluation is present, a deeper qualitative error analysis is needed. What types of relationships/entities does TeQoDO fail to capture? What are the most common errors? Understanding these failure modes would be valuable.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While the adapted evaluation metrics from Lo et al. are reasonable, they primarily focus on surface-level similarity.  A more nuanced evaluation that considers the *usability* of the generated ontologies in real-world applications (e.g., information retrieval, question answering) would strengthen the claims. The downstream DST task is a positive step but limited.
    *   **Oversimplification:** The reliance on the 'is_successful' (dialogue success) signal might be an oversimplification. Real-world dialogues are far more complex, and a simple binary success indicator may not adequately capture the nuances of user goals and system performance.
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4o:** The method relies heavily on a powerful closed-source LLM (GPT-4o). This limits reproducibility and raises concerns about the generalizability of the approach to other LLMs.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the field by:
    *   Inspiring more research on automated ontology construction using LLMs.
    *   Encouraging the use of structured outputs (like SQL) for knowledge representation.
    *   Highlighting the importance of incorporating dialogue theory into LLM-based dialogue systems.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the moderate novelty, promising performance, the scalability demonstrated (albeit with qualifications), and the contribution to LLM explainability, but also considering the SQL limitations, prompt sensitivity, reliance on GPT-4o, and the need for more in-depth error analysis and real-world validation, a score of 6 is appropriate. While the idea is good and the results are promising, several limitations need to be addressed before TeQoDO can be considered a truly groundbreaking contribution. It's a solid step in the right direction but requires further refinement and more comprehensive validation.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Trae Agent: An LLM-based Agent for Software Engineering with Test-time Scaling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23370v1)
- **Authors**: Trae Research Team, Pengfei Gao, Zhao Tian, Xiangxin Meng, Xinchen Wang, Ruida Hu, Yuanan Xiao, Yizhou Liu, Zhao Zhang, Junjie Chen, Cuiyun Gao, Yun Lin, Yingfei Xiong, Chao Peng, Xia Liu
- **Abstract**: Software issue resolution is a critical challenge in software engineering and has garnered increasing attention in recent years. With the rapid advancement of large language models (LLMs), substantial progress has been made in addressing real-world software engineering tasks. Recent studies have introduced ensemble reasoning techniques to enhance the performance of LLM-based issue resolution. However, existing prompting-based methods still face limitations in effectively exploring large ensemble spaces and lack the capacity for repository-level understanding, both of which constrain their overall effectiveness. In this paper, we propose Trae Agent, the first agent-based ensemble reasoning approach for repository-level issue resolution. Trae Agent formulates our goal as an optimal solution search problem and addresses two key challenges, i.e., large ensemble spaces and repository-level understanding, through modular agents for generation, pruning, and selection. We conduct extensive experiments using three leading LLMs on the widely-adopted SWE-bench benchmark, comparing Trae Agent against four state-of-the-art ensemble reasoning techniques. Experimental results demonstrate that Trae Agent consistently achieves superior performance, with an average improvement of 10.22% over all baselines in terms of Pass@1. Trae Agent has achieved first place on the SWE-bench Verified leaderboard, with a notable Pass@1 score of 75.20%. We are pleased to release Trae Agent as an open-source project to support the research community, with all resources available at https://github.com/bytedance/trae-agent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Trae Agent: An LLM-based Agent for Software Engineering with Test-time Scaling" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Trae Agent, a novel agent-based ensemble reasoning framework for resolving software issues at the repository level. It addresses the limitations of existing prompting-based methods, which struggle with large ensemble spaces and lack repository-level understanding. Trae Agent uses a modular architecture with three key components: patch generation (using a novel coder agent), patch pruning (hierarchical, combining deduplication and regression testing), and patch selection (simulating a real-world program comprehension process).  Experiments on the SWE-bench benchmark using Gemini 2.5 Pro, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and GPT-4.1 demonstrate that Trae Agent outperforms state-of-the-art ensemble reasoning baselines. The agent achieves a high Pass@1 score and demonstrates strong performance across various ensemble sizes. The authors provide extensive ablation studies to validate the contribution of each component. The project is released as open-source.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the **agent-based architecture for ensemble reasoning** within the software engineering domain, particularly for issue resolution. While ensemble methods and LLM agents for software engineering have been explored, Trae Agent integrates them in a unique and effective way. The **hierarchical patch pruning** strategy combining deduplication and regression testing is another novel contribution. The program comprehension inspired patch selection is also worth mentioning. While components are not entirely individually novel the architecture certainly represents a valuable aggregation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in software engineering: automated software issue resolution. The demonstrated performance improvements over existing methods on a standardized benchmark (SWE-bench) suggests practical significance. The open-source release further amplifies its potential impact by enabling other researchers to build upon and extend the work. The reported high number of GitHub stars indicates significant community interest.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed, employing multiple LLMs, a recognized benchmark, and ablation studies.
    *   **Clear Architecture:** The modular design makes the framework easy to understand and potentially extend.
    *   **Open-Source Release:**  Promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Significant performance boost:** Consistently surpasses the best alternative methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Benchmarks:** The evaluation is primarily focused on SWE-bench, which, as acknowledged by the authors, can be noisy. While the use of SWE-bench Verified mitigates this, expanding the evaluation to more diverse and real-world scenarios would further strengthen the results.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges the trade-off between effectiveness and computational cost with larger ensemble sizes.  However, a more detailed analysis of the computational requirements of Trae Agent compared to baselines would be valuable.
    *   **Limited Exploration of LLM Synergies:** While the Mixture setting introduces some diversity, further investigation into how different LLMs might be optimally combined within the agent architecture could lead to further improvements.

*   **Potential Influence:** Trae Agent has the potential to influence research in several areas:
    *   **Agent-Based Software Engineering:** Provides a blueprint for building more sophisticated agents for complex software engineering tasks.
    *   **Ensemble Reasoning with LLMs:** Demonstrates the benefits of ensemble reasoning and highlights the importance of effective patch pruning and selection strategies.
    *   **Automated Issue Resolution:** Contributes to the ongoing effort to automate software issue resolution and reduce developer burden.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite the minor weaknesses, the paper's novelty, thorough evaluation, and open-source release make it a significant contribution to the field. The agent-based architecture with the unique incorporation of ensemble reasoning with a coder, tester, and selector agent provides a valuable insight into solving a prominent issue in the SE domain. The significant performance boost over SOTA techniques is also worth emphasizing.

### UniEmo: Unifying Emotional Understanding and Generation with Learnable Expert Queries
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23372v1)
- **Authors**: Yijie Zhu, Lingsen Zhang, Zitong Yu, Rui Shao, Tao Tan, Liqiang Nie
- **Abstract**: Emotional understanding and generation are often treated as separate tasks, yet they are inherently complementary and can mutually enhance each other. In this paper, we propose the UniEmo, a unified framework that seamlessly integrates these two tasks. The key challenge lies in the abstract nature of emotions, necessitating the extraction of visual representations beneficial for both tasks. To address this, we propose a hierarchical emotional understanding chain with learnable expert queries that progressively extracts multi-scale emotional features, thereby serving as a foundational step for unification. Simultaneously, we fuse these expert queries and emotional representations to guide the diffusion model in generating emotion-evoking images. To enhance the diversity and fidelity of the generated emotional images, we further introduce the emotional correlation coefficient and emotional condition loss into the fusion process. This step facilitates fusion and alignment for emotional generation guided by the understanding. In turn, we demonstrate that joint training allows the generation component to provide implicit feedback to the understanding part. Furthermore, we propose a novel data filtering algorithm to select high-quality and diverse emotional images generated by the well-trained model, which explicitly feedback into the understanding part. Together, these generation-driven dual feedback processes enhance the model's understanding capacity. Extensive experiments show that UniEmo significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both emotional understanding and generation tasks. The code for the proposed method is available at https://github.com/JiuTian-VL/UniEmo.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the UniEmo paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UniEmo, a unified framework that integrates visual emotion understanding and generation. The core idea revolves around extracting richer emotional representations using a hierarchical emotional understanding chain with learnable expert queries. These queries, focusing on both scene and object levels, are fused with emotional representations to guide a diffusion model in generating emotion-evoking images. The framework employs an emotional correlation coefficient to improve fusion and an emotional condition loss to align features with the target emotion space. Additionally, the paper presents a novel data filtering algorithm to select high-quality generated images, which are then fed back into the understanding component, creating a dual feedback loop to enhance the model's understanding capacity. Experiments demonstrate that UniEmo outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both emotion understanding and generation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The concept of unifying emotional understanding and generation is not entirely new; however, UniEmo presents a novel approach by using learnable expert queries to extract hierarchical, multi-scale emotional features and creating a closed-loop feedback system between understanding and generation. This query-based approach applied to emotion, distinguishing scene from object features, represents a noteworthy contribution. The introduction of the emotional correlation coefficient for effective fusion and the explicit feedback mechanism using a carefully designed data filtering algorithm are also significant additions.  The paper builds upon existing techniques (diffusion models, ViT), but the integrated architecture and novel components are the key differentiator.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to advance the field of affective computing. By creating a unified framework, UniEmo provides a way to enhance both emotion understanding and generation. Better emotion understanding can lead to improved human-computer interaction, mental health applications, and entertainment technologies.  Improved emotion-conditioned image generation is beneficial for virtual environments, digital media, and marketing. The dual feedback mechanism is particularly promising, suggesting a pathway for self-improving models that learn to understand and generate emotional content more effectively over time.  The experimental results, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on multiple datasets, provide strong evidence for the practical value of the proposed approach.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem and solution:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of emotional understanding and generation and proposes a well-structured and comprehensive solution.
    *   **Innovative approach:**  The use of learnable expert queries and the dual feedback mechanism represents a significant innovation in the field.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The paper presents extensive experimental results on multiple datasets, demonstrating the superior performance of UniEmo compared to existing methods.
    *   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, making it accessible to a broad audience.
    *   **Code available:**  The availability of code enhances the reproducibility of the research and facilitates its adoption by other researchers.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on existing architectures:** The paper relies on existing architectures such as ViT and diffusion models. While the authors introduce novel components, the core framework is not entirely new.
    *   **Computational cost:** The learnable expert queries and joint training process may increase the computational cost of the model. A deeper discussion of the trade-off between performance and computational cost would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited ablation studies:** While there are ablation studies, further investigation into the specific contributions of each component would be valuable.  For instance, more detailed analyses of the effect of different loss functions or query lengths could reveal further insights.
    *   **Potential biases:** The training datasets used in the paper may contain biases that could affect the performance of the model on different populations or cultures. A discussion of these potential biases would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** UniEmo has the potential to significantly influence the field of affective computing by establishing a new approach to emotional understanding and generation. The learnable expert queries and dual feedback mechanism could be adopted by other researchers in the field. The paper's clear and well-documented implementation should facilitate its adoption and extension.

**Justification of Score:**

Despite the reliance on existing architectures and the potential for further ablation studies, the paper introduces significant novel components and demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on challenging benchmarks. The combination of learnable expert queries, emotional correlation coefficient, emotional condition loss, and the dual feedback mechanism represents a significant advancement in the field. The potential influence of UniEmo on future research in affective computing is substantial. Therefore, a score of 7.5 is appropriate.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM4Rail: An LLM-Augmented Railway Service Consulting Platform
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23377v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuo Li, Xianghuai Deng, Chiwei Feng, Hanmeng Li, Shenjie Wang, Haichao Zhang, Teng Jia, Conlin Chen, Louis Linchun Wu, Jia Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have significantly reshaped different walks of business. To meet the increasing demands for individualized railway service, we develop LLM4Rail - a novel LLM-augmented railway service consulting platform. Empowered by LLM, LLM4Rail can provide custom modules for ticketing, railway food & drink recommendations, weather information, and chitchat. In LLM4Rail, we propose the iterative "Question-Thought-Action-Observation (QTAO)" prompting framework. It meticulously integrates verbal reasoning with task-oriented actions, that is, reasoning to guide action selection, to effectively retrieve external observations relevant to railway operation and service to generate accurate responses. To provide personalized onboard dining services, we first construct the Chinese Railway Food and Drink (CRFD-25) - a publicly accessible takeout dataset tailored for railway services. CRFD-25 covers a wide range of signature dishes categorized by cities, cuisines, age groups, and spiciness levels. We further introduce an LLM-based zero-shot conversational recommender for railway catering. To address the unconstrained nature of open recommendations, the feature similarity-based post-processing step is introduced to ensure all the recommended items are aligned with CRFD-25 dataset.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LLM4Rail: An LLM-Augmented Railway Service Consulting Platform."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM4Rail, a novel platform designed to improve railway services by leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs).  LLM4Rail aims to provide personalized services like ticketing, food/drink recommendations, weather updates, and general conversation within the Railway 12306 App.  The core contribution is the "Question-Thought-Action-Observation (QTAO)" prompting framework, which iteratively guides the LLM through reasoning, action selection, external tool retrieval (e.g., weather API, ticket database), and observation of results to generate accurate responses.  To personalize food/drink recommendations, the authors created the Chinese Railway Food & Drink (CRFD-25) dataset, a railway-specific takeout dataset with diverse dishes categorized by city, cuisine, and other features.  Finally, LLM4Rail implements a zero-shot conversational recommender with feature similarity matching to align recommendations with the CRFD-25 dataset.  The paper presents experimental results comparing different foundation models (Qwen3, GPT-40, Gemini-2.5-pro) and evaluating the performance of various components.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing concepts, but the overall integration and specific application to the railway domain exhibit some novelty. The QTAO framework is inspired by ReAct, but its tailoring and use in conjunction with railway-specific tools can be considered a reasonable advancement. The CRFD-25 dataset is a practical contribution, filling a gap in publicly available datasets for this particular domain. The idea of a zero-shot recommender fine-tuned by feature alignment of candidate dishes is also novel.

*   **Significance:** Improving railway services and passenger experiences is an area of real-world value. Applying LLMs to this sector has the potential to automate tasks, personalize interactions, and increase efficiency. The CRFD-25 dataset could be beneficial to other researchers in the field. However, the significance is somewhat limited by the scope, primarily focused on the Chinese railway system. Generalizability of the specific solutions to other contexts might require further research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-structured and clearly written:** The paper presents the problem, proposed solution, and experimental results in a logical and easy-to-understand manner.
    *   **Practical application:** LLM4Rail demonstrates a tangible application of LLMs to a real-world problem.
    *   **Dataset creation:** The CRFD-25 dataset contributes a valuable resource for railway service research.
    *   **QTAO Framework:** The adaptation and implementation of QTAO show a practical approach to integrating LLMs with external tools.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of elements is novel, each individual component (QTAO, zero-shot recommender, feature similarity) builds upon existing techniques.  The key novelty lies in the railway application and associated dataset.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation is somewhat narrow, focusing primarily on accuracy and the number of QTAO iterations.  More detailed analysis of user satisfaction, efficiency gains, and potential biases would strengthen the claims. There are no human studies to corroborate the quality of the system from a users point of view.
    *   **Generalizability:** The platform appears to be tailored specifically for the Chinese railway system, which might limit its broader applicability. The recommendation dataset is tailored to specific Chinese foods, which limits generalization.
    *   **Limited depth on specific sub-modules**: For example, the feature similarity mapping method in recommendation is not elaborated, which should be if it is meant to be a novel addition. Similarly, the lack of implementation details to the method of data annotation for the CRFD dataset makes the novelty limited.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could inspire further research on applying LLMs to other niche domains and industries. The QTAO framework and CRFD-25 dataset could serve as useful starting points for future projects. However, its limited generalizability might restrict its influence to the Chinese railway service context.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a moderately novel and significant contribution. While the core techniques are not groundbreaking, the practical application to the railway domain, the creation of the CRFD-25 dataset, and the integration of the QTAO framework demonstrate worthwhile engineering effort. The weaknesses related to evaluation, generalizability, and lack of depth weaken the paper's impact. I believe that a score of 6 reflects a fair assessment of its overall contribution.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### MPCC: A Novel Benchmark for Multimodal Planning with Complex Constraints in Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23382v1)
- **Authors**: Yiyan Ji, Haoran Chen, Qiguang Chen, Chengyue Wu, Libo Qin, Wanxiang Che
- **Abstract**: Multimodal planning capabilities refer to the ability to predict, reason, and design steps for task execution with multimodal context, which is essential for complex reasoning and decision-making across multiple steps. However, current benchmarks face two key challenges: (1) they cannot directly assess multimodal real-world planning capabilities, and (2) they lack constraints or implicit constraints across modalities. To address these issues, we introduce Multimodal Planning with Complex Constraints (MPCC), the first benchmark to systematically evaluate MLLMs' ability to handle multimodal constraints in planning. To address the first challenge, MPCC focuses on three real-world tasks: Flight Planning, Calendar Planning, and Meeting Planning. To solve the second challenge, we introduce complex constraints (e.g. budget, temporal, and spatial) in these tasks, with graded difficulty levels (EASY, MEDIUM, HARD) to separate constraint complexity from search space expansion. Experiments on 13 advanced MLLMs reveal significant challenges: closed-source models achieve only 21.3% feasible plans, while open-source models average below 11%. Additionally, we observe that MLLMs are highly sensitive to constraint complexity and that traditional multimodal prompting strategies fail in multi-constraint scenarios. Our work formalizes multimodal constraints in planning, provides a rigorous evaluation framework, and highlights the need for advancements in constraint-aware reasoning for real-world MLLM applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MPCC, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the multimodal planning capabilities of large language models (MLLMs) under complex constraints.  Unlike existing benchmarks that focus primarily on expanding the search space or improving multimodal perception, MPCC emphasizes the satisfaction of intricate constraints across modalities, reflecting the complexity of real-world planning scenarios. The benchmark includes three real-world tasks: Flight Planning, Calendar Planning, and Meeting Planning, each with graded difficulty levels and a focus on budget, temporal, and spatial constraints. The paper details the dataset creation process, constraint modeling, and evaluation metrics. Experiments on a range of MLLMs reveal significant challenges, particularly concerning constraint sensitivity and the limitations of traditional prompting strategies. The authors argue that their benchmark formalizes multimodal constraints in planning and provides a rigorous evaluation framework for the advancement of constraint-aware reasoning in real-world MLLM applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in its explicit focus on *complex constraints* within multimodal planning tasks. While existing benchmarks address multimodal planning, they often emphasize search space expansion or improved perception.  MPCC's focus on budget, temporal, and spatial constraints, and their interaction across modalities, is a distinct and valuable contribution. The explicit definition and categorization of these constraints provide a more structured framework for evaluating MLLM performance. However, similar to existing datasets, MPCC relies on synthetic or procedurally generated datasets which are not a perfect substitute for real-world complexity. While the authors state they used real-world interface frames, the task structures are still generated.

*   **Significance:** The benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating MLLMs for real-world applications. Many real-world planning problems inherently involve satisfying complex constraints (e.g., booking flights within a budget while adhering to time constraints). By highlighting the weaknesses of current MLLMs in this area, MPCC encourages researchers to develop more robust constraint-aware reasoning mechanisms.  The negative results reported across multiple MLLMs underscores that current approaches are not sufficient, even with closed-source models. The analysis of prompting strategies and error types further provides valuable insights for future research directions. The systematic construction of the dataset with graded difficulty levels is also a significant strength, allowing for a more fine-grained analysis of model performance under different constraint complexities. The paper identifies a strong opportunity for future development of multimodal planning in LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Definition of Multimodal Constraints:**  The paper clearly formalizes the concept of multimodal constraints and categorizes them effectively.
    *   **Real-World Relevance:**  The selected planning tasks (Flight, Calendar, Meeting) are highly relevant to real-world scenarios.
    *   **Graded Difficulty Levels:**  The graded difficulty levels provide a systematic way to evaluate model performance under increasing complexity.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:** The evaluation across multiple MLLMs and prompting strategies provides a comprehensive assessment of the current state-of-the-art.
    *   **Error Analysis:**  The manual error analysis offers valuable insights into the types of failures commonly encountered by MLLMs.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Synthetic Data:** While the paper utilizes interfaces similar to real-world applications, the core data within the tasks is procedurally generated. This may not fully capture the nuances and complexities of real-world planning problems. Although they filter using pre-labeling, it might not be enough to approximate complex real-world scenarios.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Mitigation Strategies:** While the paper highlights the limitations of existing prompting strategies, it does not delve deeply into potential mitigation techniques or offer new solutions.
    *   **Benchmark Scope:** The benchmark focuses on a relatively small set of tasks, which may limit its generalizability to other planning domains.

*   **Potential Influence:** MPCC has the potential to become a valuable resource for researchers working on multimodal reasoning and planning. It can serve as a standardized benchmark for evaluating and comparing new MLLM architectures and training techniques. The insights from the error analysis can guide the development of more robust constraint-aware reasoning mechanisms.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

MPCC presents a valuable and novel contribution by focusing on complex constraints in multimodal planning. While the reliance on synthetic data and the limited scope of tasks are weaknesses, the benchmark addresses a critical gap in current MLLM evaluation. It provides a well-defined framework, systematic evaluation, and insightful error analysis that can drive future research. For these reasons, the paper warrants a score of 7 reflecting its solid contribution, with room for improvement through incorporating more real-world data and expanding the scope of the tasks.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Causal2Vec: Improving Decoder-only LLMs as Versatile Embedding Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23386v1)
- **Authors**: Ailiang Lin, Zhuoyun Li, Kotaro Funakoshi
- **Abstract**: Decoder-only large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to build embedding models that effectively encode the semantic information of natural language texts into dense vector representations for various embedding tasks. However, many existing methods primarily focus on removing the causal attention mask in LLMs to enable bidirectional attention, potentially undermining the model's ability to extract semantic information acquired during pretraining. Additionally, leading unidirectional approaches often rely on extra input text to overcome the inherent limitations of causal attention, inevitably increasing computational costs. In this work, we propose Causal2Vec, a general-purpose embedding model tailored to enhance the performance of decoder-only LLMs without altering their original architectures or introducing significant computational overhead. Specifically, we first employ a lightweight BERT-style model to pre-encode the input text into a single Contextual token, which is then prepended to the LLM's input sequence, allowing each token to capture contextualized information even without attending to future tokens. Furthermore, to mitigate the recency bias introduced by last-token pooling and help LLMs better leverage the semantic information encoded in the Contextual token, we concatenate the last hidden states of Contextual and EOS tokens as the final text embedding. In practice, Causal2Vec achieves state-of-the-art performance on the Massive Text Embeddings Benchmark (MTEB) among models trained solely on publicly available retrieval datasets, while reducing the required sequence length by up to 85% and inference time by up to 82% compared to best-performing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Causal2Vec: Improving Decoder-only LLMs as Versatile Embedding Models" introduces a method to enhance the performance of decoder-only large language models (LLMs) for text embedding tasks.  The key idea is to prepend a "Contextual token" to the LLM's input sequence. This token is generated by a lightweight, off-the-shelf BERT-style model and is meant to capture the contextual content of the entire input text.  Additionally, the paper proposes concatenating the last hidden states of the Contextual token and the EOS token as the final text embedding, aiming to mitigate recency bias associated with last-token pooling. The method, called Causal2Vec, doesn't require modifications to the LLM architecture itself and aims to improve performance without significantly increasing computational overhead. Experiments on the MTEB benchmark demonstrate state-of-the-art results among models trained solely on public retrieval data.  The paper also highlights reduced sequence length and inference time compared to other methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper has moderate novelty. The idea of using an auxiliary model (BERT) to enrich the input to a primary model (decoder-only LLM) is not entirely new. Existing works use prompt examples.  However, the specific combination of using a BERT-style model to create a single "Contextual token," prepending it, and then using a concatenated hidden state representation is relatively novel. The paper also builds upon existing work on causal attention limitation. The specific method of circumventing this, without changing the LLM structure, provides incremental contribution.

* **Significance:** The significance lies in the practical benefits of the proposed method:
    * **Improved Performance:** The method demonstrates improved performance on the MTEB benchmark compared to several baselines (trained on similar dataset), particularly concerning other methods using original LLM's attention mechanism.
    * **Efficiency:** The reduced sequence length and inference time are significant advantages, making the method more suitable for resource-constrained scenarios. It is able to compete with methods (bge-en-icl) that are known to be effective.
    * **Ease of Adoption:**  A major strength is the minimal modification to the core LLM architecture. This facilitates easy adoption across different decoder-only LLMs without the need for complex code modifications, broadening usability by the community.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper includes extensive experiments and ablation studies on a standard benchmark.
    * **Clear and Well-Motivated Method:** The problem is clearly defined, the proposed solution is well-explained, and the design choices are justified.
    * **Focus on Practicality:** The method is designed for practical deployment, with a focus on efficiency and ease of integration.
    * **Comprehensive Analysis:** The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of each component of the proposed method.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Innovation:** The method builds on existing concepts and doesn't represent a groundbreaking theoretical advance. It is rather an "engineering" advance.
    * **Limited LLM Scope:** The evaluation is limited to three LLMs up to 7B parameters.  While useful, evaluations on larger LLMs are desirable to validate the method's scalability and effectiveness further. The gains are not guaranteed on larger models.
    * **BERT Dependence:** Performance relies on the quality of the external BERT model used. Although it highlights using an off-the-shelf BERT, this dependency somewhat compromises the end-to-end nature of leveraging LLM embedding. Also it is somewhat limiting using solely publicly available retrieval datasets when other models use non-public or synthetic datasets, making a direct comparison more difficult and the full potential may not be explored.
    * **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While there's intuitive justification, a deeper theoretical analysis of *why* this approach works would strengthen the paper.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of text embedding, particularly for those working with decoder-only LLMs. The simplicity, efficiency, and strong empirical results could make Causal2Vec a popular choice for improving the performance of LLMs in various downstream applications, including RAG.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid, well-executed method that addresses a practical problem in adapting decoder-only LLMs for text embedding. The focus on efficiency and ease of adoption is valuable, and the empirical results are convincing. However, the novelty is incremental, and the analysis could be deepened with theoretical insights and broader LLM evaluations.  The strength of empirical work, and practical usage, means it receives a moderately high score.
