### Beyond Gloss: A Hand-Centric Framework for Gloss-Free Sign Language Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23575v1)
- **Authors**: Sobhan Asasi, Mohamed Ilyas Lakhal, Ozge Mercanoglu Sincan, Richard Bowden
- **Abstract**: Sign Language Translation (SLT) is a challenging task that requires bridging the modality gap between visual and linguistic information while capturing subtle variations in hand shapes and movements. To address these challenges, we introduce \textbf{BeyondGloss}, a novel gloss-free SLT framework that leverages the spatio-temporal reasoning capabilities of Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs). Since existing VideoLLMs struggle to model long videos in detail, we propose a novel approach to generate fine-grained, temporally-aware textual descriptions of hand motion. A contrastive alignment module aligns these descriptions with video features during pre-training, encouraging the model to focus on hand-centric temporal dynamics and distinguish signs more effectively. To further enrich hand-specific representations, we distill fine-grained features from HaMeR. Additionally, we apply a contrastive loss between sign video representations and target language embeddings to reduce the modality gap in pre-training. \textbf{BeyondGloss} achieves state-of-the-art performance on the Phoenix14T and CSL-Daily benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed framework. We will release the code upon acceptance of the paper.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "BeyondGloss," a new framework for gloss-free sign language translation (SLT). It aims to improve upon existing methods by focusing on hand-centric information and leveraging the power of Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs). The key components of the framework are: 1) a novel method for generating fine-grained, temporally-aware textual descriptions of hand motions in sign videos using VideoLLMs; 2) a contrastive alignment module that aligns these descriptions with video features during pre-training; 3) feature distillation from Hand Mesh Recovery (HaMeR) to enrich hand-specific representations; and 4) a contrastive loss between sign video representations and target language embeddings. The paper claims state-of-the-art performance on the Phoenix14T and CSL-Daily benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of its hand-centric approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a potentially valuable contribution to the field of gloss-free SLT, but there are strengths and weaknesses that influence its overall novelty and significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Hand-Centric Approach:** Explicitly focusing on hand movements is a logical and promising direction for improving SLT, given the hands' central role in sign languages. By utilizing haMeR it guides the model towards hand pose and orientation.
    *   **VideoLLM Integration:** The innovative approach of using VideoLLMs to generate hand-centric descriptions to add semantic information and aligned visual content.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper presents experimental results and ablation studies on two widely used datasets (Phoenix14T and CSL-Daily)

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on External Models:** The reliance on pre-trained models like HaMeR and VideoLLMs may limit the generalizability of the approach to languages where those resources are less readily available or less effective. There may be a dependency on the prompt engineering to utilize VideoLLMs. The paper does not show the effects of generating various prompts.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the paper claims state-of-the-art results, the performance gains compared to previous methods (LLaVA-SLT) may be considered incremental.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While the paper includes qualitative examples, a more in-depth analysis of the model's strengths and weaknesses in different scenarios would be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of In-depth Error Analysis:** An analysis of the types of errors the model still makes would provide valuable insights for future research.
    *   **Computational cost:** Utilising VideoLLMs will definitely add the computational cost to the approach.

**Novelty:** The use of video large language models to create descriptions for the sign video is novel. Adding the additional distillation loss from haMeR is also a good direction.

**Significance:** Improving gloss-free SLT is significant because it makes SLT more accessible for languages where gloss annotations are not available. The proposed hand-centric approach is a reasonable direction, although further research will be needed to fully realize the potential of the idea.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper has some novelty, the work utilizes VideoLLMs to generate textual data for sign videos. Adding haMeR distillation loss is also a smart move. I rated it less than the maximum because of the limitations described above.

**Score: 6.5**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### DiffLoRA: Differential Low-Rank Adapters for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23588v1)
- **Authors**: Alexandre Misrahi, Nadezhda Chirkova, Maxime Louis, Vassilina Nikoulina
- **Abstract**: Differential Transformer has recently been proposed to improve performance in Transformer models by canceling out noise through a denoiser attention mechanism. In this work, we introduce DiffLoRA, a parameter-efficient adaptation of the differential attention mechanism, with low-rank adapters on both positive and negative attention terms. This approach retains the efficiency of LoRA while aiming to benefit from the performance gains of differential attention. We evaluate DiffLoRA across a broad range of NLP tasks, including general benchmarks, many-shot in-context learning, RAG, and long-context tests. We observe that, although DiffLoRA falls short of other parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods in most evaluation tasks, it shows interesting results in certain domains (+11 pts on LoRA for HumanEval). We analyze the attention patterns post-finetuning to identify the reasons for this behavior.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DiffLoRA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffLoRA, a parameter-efficient adaptation technique that incorporates the differential attention mechanism (DiffAttn) into pre-trained large language models (LLMs). DiffAttn, originally designed to improve Transformer performance by suppressing noise, is integrated using low-rank adapters (LoRA) on both the positive and negative attention terms. The authors evaluate DiffLoRA on a diverse range of NLP tasks, including general benchmarks, few-shot learning, retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), and long-context scenarios. While DiffLoRA doesn't consistently outperform other parameter-efficient methods across all tasks, it demonstrates interesting results in specific domains. The paper also analyzes the attention patterns after fine-tuning to understand the observed behaviors.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of combining Differential Transformer's attention mechanism with LoRA for parameter-efficient adaptation is a relatively novel approach. It attempts to bring the benefits of differential attention, primarily seen in training from scratch, to the realm of efficient fine-tuning of pre-trained models. However, the core components (DiffAttn and LoRA) are well-established, so the primary novelty lies in their specific combination.

*   **Significance:** The significance is somewhat limited by the empirical results. While the concept is interesting, DiffLoRA doesn't consistently demonstrate superior performance compared to established methods like LoRA across a broad range of tasks. The inconsistent performance indicates that the simple combination might not be directly advantageous, or requires further optimization and design choices. The improvement in specific domains, such as the HumanEval dataset is noteworthy but insufficient to establish a broad significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an important problem:** Parameter-efficient adaptation of LLMs is a crucial research area, and exploring ways to incorporate architectural innovations like DiffAttn is worthwhile.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper evaluates DiffLoRA across a wide variety of tasks, providing a good overview of its performance in different settings. This allows for a solid evaluation of the approach's strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Attention pattern analysis:** The analysis of attention patterns after fine-tuning offers some insights into the model's behavior and provides potential avenues for further improvement.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Inconsistent Performance:** DiffLoRA does not consistently outperform LoRA or the base model. This raises concerns about the general applicability and effectiveness of the method.
    *   **Limited Insights:** While the paper analyzes attention patterns, it doesn't provide deep insights into why DiffLoRA performs well in some tasks and poorly in others. The analysis feels somewhat superficial and doesn't fully explain the underlying mechanisms.
    *   **Lack of Optimization:** The paper presents DiffLoRA as a straightforward combination of DiffAttn and LoRA. It lacks evidence of significant optimization efforts to tailor the method to the pre-trained model or the target tasks. Simply applying LoRA to DiffAttn might not be sufficient to realize its full potential.
    *   **Alternative explanation:** Given the context of efficient finetuning it could be argued that the introduction of more learnable parameter simply overfits to the tuning set, in which case DiffLoRA should be regularized.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper might encourage further research into combining architectural innovations with parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods. It highlights the challenges of directly transferring techniques designed for training from scratch to the fine-tuning setting. However, given the mixed results, its direct impact on the field might be limited unless follow-up work addresses the identified weaknesses.

**Score: 4**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel combination of existing techniques (DiffAttn and LoRA) for parameter-efficient fine-tuning of LLMs. However, the inconsistent empirical results and lack of significant performance improvements compared to baseline methods significantly limit its impact. While the attention pattern analysis provides some insights, it doesn't fully explain the model's behavior. The paper lacks evidence of significant optimization efforts and suffers from an overall lack of depth in its analysis and justification of results. While the approach has the potential to influence future research, the current implementation falls short of demonstrating significant novelty or practical utility, making a high score unwarranted.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 4/10

### Can LLM-Reasoning Models Replace Classical Planning? A Benchmark Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23589v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Goebel, Patrik Zips
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models have sparked interest in their potential for robotic task planning. While these models demonstrate strong generative capabilities, their effectiveness in producing structured and executable plans remains uncertain. This paper presents a systematic evaluation of a broad spectrum of current state of the art language models, each directly prompted using Planning Domain Definition Language domain and problem files, and compares their planning performance with the Fast Downward planner across a variety of benchmarks. In addition to measuring success rates, we assess how faithfully the generated plans translate into sequences of actions that can actually be executed, identifying both strengths and limitations of using these models in this setting. Our findings show that while the models perform well on simpler planning tasks, they continue to struggle with more complex scenarios that require precise resource management, consistent state tracking, and strict constraint compliance. These results underscore fundamental challenges in applying language models to robotic planning in real world environments. By outlining the gaps that emerge during execution, we aim to guide future research toward combined approaches that integrate language models with classical planners in order to enhance the reliability and scalability of planning in autonomous robotics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Can LLM-Reasoning Models Replace Classical Planning? A Benchmark Study."

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) to replace classical symbolic planners (like Fast Downward) for robotic task planning. The authors perform a systematic evaluation comparing nine different LLMs against Fast Downward across five PDDL domains with varying complexities. They not only measure success rates but also assess "execution fidelity," which quantifies how well the generated plans translate into executable actions. The results indicate that while LLMs can perform reasonably well on simpler planning tasks, they struggle with more complex scenarios requiring precise resource management, state tracking, and constraint compliance. The paper highlights the limitations of LLMs as standalone planners and suggests future research should focus on hybrid approaches integrating LLMs with classical planning techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the general idea of using LLMs for planning isn't entirely new, this paper provides a valuable and well-structured empirical study. The assessment of execution fidelity is a particularly noteworthy contribution, highlighting a critical gap between LLM-generated plans and actual robotic execution. The specific selection of recent LLMs and the comparison across multiple PDDL domains add to the novelty. However, the core methodology of prompting LLMs with PDDL problems is not groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it provides a realistic assessment of the current capabilities of LLMs in a structured planning environment. It moves beyond simply showcasing the potential of LLMs and focuses on identifying their weaknesses and the challenges they face when applied to robotic planning. The emphasis on execution fidelity is vital as it directly relates to the practical applicability of LLM-based planning. The identification of domains where LLMs struggle most contributes to focusing future research efforts. The clear comparison with a well-established classical planner provides a solid benchmark.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Systematic and well-defined experimental setup.
    *   Use of multiple LLMs representing the current state of the art.
    *   Evaluation across diverse PDDL domains with varying complexity.
    *   Emphasis on execution fidelity as a critical evaluation metric.
    *   Clear and concise presentation of results.
    *   Insightful discussion of limitations and future research directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The prompt engineering, while likely well-considered, is not extensively described or varied. The sensitivity of LLMs to prompt design could affect the observed results.
    *   The study does not deeply investigate *why* LLMs fail in certain domains. A more in-depth error analysis could provide greater insights.
    *   Limited exploration of hybrid approaches. While the paper advocates for them, it doesn't include any experiments with such methods.
    *   The benchmark domains, while standard, are somewhat limited in their complexity and realism compared to actual robotic environments.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research by highlighting the importance of execution fidelity in LLM-based planning and by pointing out the specific domains where LLMs need improvement. The well-defined benchmark and evaluation metrics could be adopted by other researchers in the field. It also encourages the development of hybrid planning approaches.

*   **Rigor:** The study is well-designed, and the results are presented in a clear and understandable manner. However, adding a wider selection of prompts to account for LLM's sensitive nature in the prompts could reinforce the conclusions.

**Overall Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by providing a structured and insightful empirical evaluation of LLMs as standalone robotic task planners. The focus on execution fidelity and the identification of limitations in complex planning scenarios are important contributions. However, the lack of exploration of hybrid approaches and the somewhat limited analysis of *why* LLMs fail prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking work.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### MoGA: 3D Generative Avatar Prior for Monocular Gaussian Avatar Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23597v1)
- **Authors**: Zijian Dong, Longteng Duan, Jie Song, Michael J. Black, Andreas Geiger
- **Abstract**: We present MoGA, a novel method to reconstruct high-fidelity 3D Gaussian avatars from a single-view image. The main challenge lies in inferring unseen appearance and geometric details while ensuring 3D consistency and realism. Most previous methods rely on 2D diffusion models to synthesize unseen views; however, these generated views are sparse and inconsistent, resulting in unrealistic 3D artifacts and blurred appearance. To address these limitations, we leverage a generative avatar model, that can generate diverse 3D avatars by sampling deformed Gaussians from a learned prior distribution. Due to the limited amount of 3D training data such a 3D model alone cannot capture all image details of unseen identities. Consequently, we integrate it as a prior, ensuring 3D consistency by projecting input images into its latent space and enforcing additional 3D appearance and geometric constraints. Our novel approach formulates Gaussian avatar creation as a model inversion process by fitting the generative avatar to synthetic views from 2D diffusion models. The generative avatar provides a meaningful initialization for model fitting, enforces 3D regularization, and helps in refining pose estimation. Experiments show that our method surpasses state-of-the-art techniques and generalizes well to real-world scenarios. Our Gaussian avatars are also inherently animatable
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper abstract, introduction, and other sections. Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MOGA, a novel method for reconstructing high-fidelity 3D Gaussian avatars from a single-view image. MOGA addresses the challenges of inferring unseen appearance and geometric details while maintaining 3D consistency and realism. It leverages a generative 3D avatar model, learned from a prior distribution of deformed Gaussians, as a powerful human body prior.  This prior is integrated with synthetic multi-view images generated by a 2D diffusion model, and a model fitting process is applied, resulting in improved avatar reconstruction quality, robustness to self-occlusion, and animatability. The generative avatar model acts as an initialization, regularizer, and pose optimizer in this process. Experiments demonstrate MOGA's superiority over state-of-the-art techniques, especially in generalizing to real-world scenarios with diverse poses and clothing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques and how they are integrated. Specifically:
    *   **Generative Avatar Prior + Multi-View Diffusion:** The core idea of using a generative 3D avatar prior, trained using Gaussian splatting, to guide and regularize the reconstruction from sparse and potentially inconsistent multi-view images hallucinated by a 2D diffusion model is a notable contribution. Previous methods tend to rely heavily on either 2D diffusion (which lacks 3D consistency) or parametric models (like SMPL, which lacks appearance detail and flexibility).
    *   **Model Fitting Framework:** Formulating avatar reconstruction as a model inversion/fitting problem, where the generative model is fit to the synthetic views and acts as a regularizer, is a solid approach. The initialization and pose optimization enhancements that are also guided by the prior are good additions.
    *   **Gaussian Avatar Representation:** Utilizing Gaussian Splatting to represent the avatar offers advantages in terms of rendering quality and efficiency. This is particularly useful for subsequent animation applications.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Improved Reconstruction Quality:** The results section indicates a noticeable improvement in reconstruction quality, 3D consistency, and detail, especially in challenging areas like self-occlusion and side views. The ability to handle diverse clothing and poses in "in-the-wild" images is also significant.
    *   **Animatability:**  The inherent animatability of the Gaussian avatar representation is a practical benefit, making the method applicable to AR/VR and other animation-related tasks.
    *   **Generalization:** The method's reported generalization capabilities to real-world images address a key limitation of previous approaches that often struggle with domain shift.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained methodology with a good balance of generative and discriminative approaches.
    *   Strong experimental results, demonstrating the advantages of the proposed approach.
    *   Addresses limitations of existing methods.
    *   The ablations provide insight on the effect of the main components.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Reliance on a pre-trained multi-view diffusion model. The performance of MOGA is likely tied to the quality of the diffusion model. While the paper claims to improve on existing methods, a direct comparison by swapping the diffusion model in another SOTA method is not presented, which would strengthen the paper.
    *   Computational cost/efficiency is not discussed extensively. Model fitting can be computationally expensive.
    *   The method depends on SMPL as a base, so while the Gaussian Splatting allows the method to represent complex structures, it may still be limited by the accuracy of SMPL estimation, especially in complex poses.

* **Overall:**

The paper presents a solid and well-executed approach for monocular 3D human reconstruction. The integration of a generative avatar prior with multi-view diffusion is a valuable contribution. While the method is not completely revolutionary and relies on several existing components (e.g., SMPL, diffusion models), the specific combination, the model fitting framework, and the demonstrable improvements in reconstruction quality, consistency, and generalization justify a high score.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** MOGA offers a significant advance in the field of single-view human reconstruction through its innovative integration of a generative 3D avatar prior. The reported experimental results demonstrate improvements over state-of-the-art methods, particularly in handling challenging scenarios like self-occlusion and real-world images.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Medical Image De-Identification Benchmark Challenge
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23608v1)
- **Authors**: Linmin Pei, Granger Sutton, Michael Rutherford, Ulrike Wagner, Tracy Nolan, Kirk Smith, Phillip Farmer, Peter Gu, Ambar Rana, Kailing Chen, Thomas Ferleman, Brian Park, Ye Wu, Jordan Kojouharov, Gargi Singh, Jon Lemon, Tyler Willis, Milos Vukadinovic, Grant Duffy, Bryan He, David Ouyang, Marco Pereanez, Daniel Samber, Derek A. Smith, Christopher Cannistraci, Zahi Fayad, David S. Mendelson, Michele Bufano, Elmar Kotter, Hamideh Haghiri, Rajesh Baidya, Stefan Dvoretskii, Klaus H. Maier-Hein, Marco Nolden, Christopher Ablett, Silvia Siggillino, Sandeep Kaushik, Hongzhu Jiang, Sihan Xie, Zhiyu Wan, Alex Michie, Simon J Doran, Angeline Aurelia Waly, Felix A. Nathaniel Liang, Humam Arshad Mustagfirin, Michelle Grace Felicia, Kuo Po Chih, Rahul Krish, Ghulam Rasool, Nidhal Bouaynaya, Nikolas Koutsoubis, Kyle Naddeo, Kartik Pandit, Tony O'Sullivan, Raj Krish, Qinyan Pan, Scott Gustafson, Benjamin Kopchick, Laura Opsahl-Ong, Andrea Olvera-Morales, Jonathan Pinney, Kathryn Johnson, Theresa Do, Juergen Klenk, Maria Diaz, Arti Singh, Rong Chai, David A. Clunie, Fred Prior, Keyvan Farahani
- **Abstract**: The de-identification (deID) of protected health information (PHI) and personally identifiable information (PII) is a fundamental requirement for sharing medical images, particularly through public repositories, to ensure compliance with patient privacy laws. In addition, preservation of non-PHI metadata to inform and enable downstream development of imaging artificial intelligence (AI) is an important consideration in biomedical research. The goal of MIDI-B was to provide a standardized platform for benchmarking of DICOM image deID tools based on a set of rules conformant to the HIPAA Safe Harbor regulation, the DICOM Attribute Confidentiality Profiles, and best practices in preservation of research-critical metadata, as defined by The Cancer Imaging Archive (TCIA). The challenge employed a large, diverse, multi-center, and multi-modality set of real de-identified radiology images with synthetic PHI/PII inserted. The MIDI-B Challenge consisted of three phases: training, validation, and test. Eighty individuals registered for the challenge. In the training phase, we encouraged participants to tune their algorithms using their in-house or public data. The validation and test phases utilized the DICOM images containing synthetic identifiers (of 216 and 322 subjects, respectively). Ten teams successfully completed the test phase of the challenge. To measure success of a rule-based approach to image deID, scores were computed as the percentage of correct actions from the total number of required actions. The scores ranged from 97.91% to 99.93%. Participants employed a variety of open-source and proprietary tools with customized configurations, large language models, and optical character recognition (OCR). In this paper we provide a comprehensive report on the MIDI-B Challenge's design, implementation, results, and lessons learned.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper describes the Medical Image De-identification Benchmark (MIDI-B) Challenge, organized by the National Cancer Institute (NCI) in collaboration with MICCAI and Sage Bionetworks.  The challenge aimed to provide a standardized platform for benchmarking DICOM image de-identification tools based on HIPAA Safe Harbor regulations, DICOM Attribute Confidentiality Profiles, and best practices for preserving research-critical metadata. The challenge used a large, diverse, multi-center, and multi-modality set of real de-identified radiology images with synthetically inserted PHI/PII.  The MIDI-B challenge consisted of training, validation, and test phases. Ten teams successfully completed the test phase. Performance was measured as the percentage of correct de-identification actions based on pre-defined rules. Participants employed various tools, including open-source and proprietary software, large language models, and optical character recognition (OCR). The paper presents a comprehensive report on the challenge's design, implementation, results, and lessons learned.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution is the description of the MIDI-B challenge itself. Establishing a standardized benchmark for DICOM de-identification is a valuable step towards advancing the field. While de-identification techniques exist, a publicly available benchmark with synthetic but realistic PHI/PII and clear evaluation metrics provides a crucial resource for researchers and developers. The synthesis of PHI/PII into real DICOM images, making the challenge more practical than simply dealing with simulated data, is commendable. The inclusion of multiple modalities and realistic data variations also enhances the practical relevance of the benchmark.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in several aspects:

    *   **Standardization:** The lack of standardized de-identification methods has hindered the sharing of medical images for research. This challenge promotes standardization and facilitates comparison of different approaches.
    *   **Benchmarking:**  Provides a mechanism to objectively evaluate the performance of de-identification tools, aiding in their development and refinement.
    *   **Accessibility:**  By using synthetically infused PHI/PII, the challenge circumvents the ethical and legal barriers associated with sharing real patient data, making the benchmark accessible to a wider audience.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The detailed description of the challenge setup, evaluation metrics, and participating methods allows for potential replication and further development of the benchmark.
    *   **Data retention emphasis**: The MIDI-B challenge considers the data utility after de-identification, a significant concern in medical imaging.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic PHI/PII:**  While synthetic data addresses privacy concerns, its realism is still a factor. Attackers may exploit patterns unique to the synthetic PHI/PII, resulting in an overestimation of de-identification performance in real-world scenarios. The paper doesn't delve deeply into the methods used to generate the synthetic data and how its realism was assessed.
    *   **Limited Public Data:** The paper admits that many datasets used to train and validate these methods are privately owned and not publicly available. While the benchmark provides a common ground, the training data remains a barrier to entry.
    *   **Depth of analysis of submissions**: The summary of submissions section (5) provides a general high-level description. More details comparing the tool performance per category would be helpful.

*   **Impact:**  The MIDI-B challenge has the potential to significantly impact the field by:

    *   Encouraging the development of more robust and reliable de-identification tools.
    *   Facilitating the creation of public repositories of medical images for research.
    *   Improving the privacy and security of patient data.
    *   Providing insights into the limitations of current de-identification techniques.

*   **Scoring Rationale:** The paper presents a well-designed challenge addressing a critical need in medical imaging research. The use of synthetic, but realistically infused PHI/PII circumvents legal and ethical hurdles while still providing a valuable testing environment. The paper clearly details the methodology and evaluation metrics. However, the dependence on synthetic data, a lack of specific information about training datasets, and the high-level description of the submissions temper the overall impact. I am deducting points for those missing factors in the paper.

### LLM-Based Identification of Infostealer Infection Vectors from Screenshots: The Case of Aurora
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23611v1)
- **Authors**: Estelle Ruellan, Eric Clay, Nicholas Ascoli
- **Abstract**: Infostealers exfiltrate credentials, session cookies, and sensitive data from infected systems. With over 29 million stealer logs reported in 2024, manual analysis and mitigation at scale are virtually unfeasible/unpractical. While most research focuses on proactive malware detection, a significant gap remains in leveraging reactive analysis of stealer logs and their associated artifacts. Specifically, infection artifacts such as screenshots, image captured at the point of compromise, are largely overlooked by the current literature. This paper introduces a novel approach leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs), more specifically gpt-4o-mini, to analyze infection screenshots to extract potential Indicators of Compromise (IoCs), map infection vectors, and track campaigns. Focusing on the Aurora infostealer, we demonstrate how LLMs can process screenshots to identify infection vectors, such as malicious URLs, installer files, and exploited software themes. Our method extracted 337 actionable URLs and 246 relevant files from 1000 screenshots, revealing key malware distribution methods and social engineering tactics. By correlating extracted filenames, URLs, and infection themes, we identified three distinct malware campaigns, demonstrating the potential of LLM-driven analysis for uncovering infection workflows and enhancing threat intelligence. By shifting malware analysis from traditional log-based detection methods to a reactive, artifact-driven approach that leverages infection screenshots, this research presents a scalable method for identifying infection vectors and enabling early intervention.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach to analyze screenshots from infostealer logs using Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically gpt-4o-mini, to identify Indicators of Compromise (IoCs), map infection vectors, and track malware campaigns. Focusing on the Aurora infostealer, the authors demonstrate that LLMs can effectively process screenshots to extract malicious URLs, installer files, and exploited software themes.  They extracted 337 actionable URLs and 246 relevant files from 1000 screenshots and identified three distinct malware campaigns through correlating filenames, URLs, and infection themes. The approach shifts from traditional log-based detection to a reactive, artifact-driven method, offering a scalable way to identify infection vectors and enable early intervention. The paper evaluates the LLM's performance and analyzes its output to uncover malware distribution methods and social engineering tactics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach. While LLMs have been used for malware analysis before, applying them specifically to screenshots from stealer logs is a relatively unexplored area. The idea of using these "crime scene" images for reactive threat intelligence is innovative.

**Significance:** The potential significance is moderate. With the increasing volume of stealer logs, manual analysis becomes infeasible.  Automating the process of extracting IoCs and mapping infection vectors from screenshots can significantly enhance threat intelligence efforts.  The paper highlights the practical application of LLMs to a real-world cybersecurity problem. The findings on the types of software targeted (cracks, gaming mods) and distribution methods (YouTube, Google Ads) are also valuable. However, the scope is limited to Aurora infostealer, and the generalizability to other malware families needs further investigation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of analyzing vast amounts of stealer log data.
*   **Novel Approach:**  The application of LLMs to analyze screenshots is a creative and promising direction.
*   **Empirical Evaluation:** The authors provide a detailed evaluation of the LLM's performance, assessing its accuracy in different aspects of screenshot analysis. The inter-coder agreement methodology strengthens confidence in the evaluations.
*   **Actionable Insights:** The extracted IoCs, mapped infection vectors, and identified malware campaigns provide actionable insights for threat intelligence and incident response.
*   **Practical Contributions:** The work introduces a practical method of leveraging artifact driven malware analysis.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Selection:** The choice of gpt-4o-mini may limit the paper's impact. Newer, more powerful LLMs exist. The performance characteristics, including speed and cost, of gpt-4o-mini, which were not highlighted, may justify this choice. The paper would be stronger with a discussion justifying LLM model choice.
*   **Limited Generalizability:** The study focuses primarily on the Aurora infostealer. The findings may not be directly applicable to other malware families with different characteristics. More research is needed to assess the generalizability of the approach.
*   **Inconsistent Browser Tab Identification:** The LLM's inconsistent performance in identifying browser tabs is a significant limitation, potentially hindering the accurate mapping of infection vectors.
*   **Dependence on Screenshot Quality:**  The approach relies heavily on the existence and quality of screenshots, which may vary depending on the malware configuration and victim activity. The paper needs to acknowledge this limitation more explicitly.
*   **Limited Automation:** The reliance on manual review for file filtering after the initial LLM analysis indicates that it is not fully automated, with a bottleneck introduced due to the human in the loop.

**Impact:**

The paper can influence the development of more effective threat intelligence techniques. It can stimulate further research on applying LLMs to analyze malware artifacts and automate threat detection. It could also lead to the development of tools and services that leverage screenshot analysis for proactive security measures.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach with practical implications for threat intelligence. However, limitations exist in the consistency of the LLM's browser tab analysis, the degree of generalizability, and the level of automation. Therefore, while the idea and execution are solid, the paper could be significantly enhanced by addressing these weaknesses.

### DivControl: Knowledge Diversion for Controllable Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23620v1)
- **Authors**: Yucheng Xie, Fu Feng, Ruixiao Shi, Jing Wang, Yong Rui, Xin Geng
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have advanced from text-to-image (T2I) to image-to-image (I2I) generation by incorporating structured inputs such as depth maps, enabling fine-grained spatial control. However, existing methods either train separate models for each condition or rely on unified architectures with entangled representations, resulting in poor generalization and high adaptation costs for novel conditions. To this end, we propose DivControl, a decomposable pretraining framework for unified controllable generation and efficient adaptation. DivControl factorizes ControlNet via SVD into basic components-pairs of singular vectors-which are disentangled into condition-agnostic learngenes and condition-specific tailors through knowledge diversion during multi-condition training. Knowledge diversion is implemented via a dynamic gate that performs soft routing over tailors based on the semantics of condition instructions, enabling zero-shot generalization and parameter-efficient adaptation to novel conditions. To further improve condition fidelity and training efficiency, we introduce a representation alignment loss that aligns condition embeddings with early diffusion features. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DivControl achieves state-of-the-art controllability with 36.4$\times$ less training cost, while simultaneously improving average performance on basic conditions. It also delivers strong zero-shot and few-shot performance on unseen conditions, demonstrating superior scalability, modularity, and transferability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DivControl paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DivControl, a novel framework for controllable image generation that aims to improve upon existing methods by addressing limitations in generalization, adaptation costs, and computational efficiency. DivControl factorizes the ControlNet architecture using Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) into condition-agnostic "learngenes" and condition-specific "tailors". During training, a dynamic gate, guided by textual instructions describing the condition, performs soft routing over the tailors, enabling the model to adapt to diverse conditions and generalize to unseen ones in a zero-shot manner. A representation alignment loss is also incorporated to align condition embeddings with early diffusion features, further improving condition fidelity and training efficiency. Experiments demonstrate that DivControl achieves state-of-the-art controllability with significantly reduced training costs compared to existing methods, while also improving average performance and demonstrating strong zero-shot and few-shot performance on unseen conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the decomposition of ControlNet using SVD coupled with the dynamic gating mechanism and the representation alignment loss.  Knowledge diversion, while not entirely new, is cleverly applied to controllable image generation within the ControlNet framework. The idea of disentangling condition-agnostic and condition-specific knowledge and dynamically activating the appropriate modules based on the textual description of the condition is a significant contribution. Existing methods like CtrLoRA address transferability but lack the dynamic routing and disentanglement achieved by DivControl.
*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from addressing a key bottleneck in controllable image generation: the high computational cost of training separate models for each condition or the poor generalization of unified architectures. DivControl tackles this problem effectively by enabling efficient adaptation to novel conditions with minimal overhead. The results showing a 36.4x reduction in training costs compared to CtrLoRA while improving performance are compelling. Furthermore, the strong zero-shot and few-shot performance on unseen conditions demonstrates the potential for a more scalable and adaptable approach to controllable generation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The demonstrated reduction in training cost is a major advantage.
    *   **Generalization:** The zero-shot and few-shot results on unseen conditions are strong and suggest good generalization capabilities.
    *   **Modularity:** The decomposition into learngenes and tailors facilitates modular reuse and potentially opens up avenues for further improvements.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and the method is clearly explained.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Textual Instructions:** The dynamic gate relies on textual instructions for conditions. While this is a strength in some ways, it also adds a dependency on the quality and availability of such instructions. The choice of a pretrained text encoder and its potential limitations are not explored deeply. Performance is dependent on good prompt engineering and robust text encoder performance.
    *   **Ablation depth:** The ablation depth is not extensively covered to assess effectiveness of REPA, more in depth studies could lead to further insight to REPA.
    *   **Qualitative assessment:** Qualitative studies can further cement confidence in novel approaches, more can be provided for DivControl.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty and significance of the paper, combined with its strengths and minor weaknesses, a score of 8 is appropriate. DivControl presents a significant advancement in controllable image generation by enabling efficient adaptation and generalization through a combination of knowledge diversion, dynamic routing, and representation alignment. The results convincingly demonstrate its superiority over existing methods in terms of computational cost and performance. The minor weaknesses related to dependence on textual instructions are not critical enough to significantly detract from the paper's overall contribution. Future research could explore alternative condition representations and further optimize the training process.

### MemoCue: Empowering LLM-Based Agents for Human Memory Recall via Strategy-Guided Querying
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23633v1)
- **Authors**: Qian Zhao, Zhuo Sun, Bin Guo, Zhiwen Yu
- **Abstract**: Agent-assisted memory recall is one critical research problem in the field of human-computer interaction. In conventional methods, the agent can retrieve information from its equipped memory module to help the person recall incomplete or vague memories. The limited size of memory module hinders the acquisition of complete memories and impacts the memory recall performance in practice. Memory theories suggest that the person's relevant memory can be proactively activated through some effective cues. Inspired by this, we propose a novel strategy-guided agent-assisted memory recall method, allowing the agent to transform an original query into a cue-rich one via the judiciously designed strategy to help the person recall memories. To this end, there are two key challenges. (1) How to choose the appropriate recall strategy for diverse forgetting scenarios with distinct memory-recall characteristics? (2) How to obtain the high-quality responses leveraging recall strategies, given only abstract and sparsely annotated strategy patterns? To address the challenges, we propose a Recall Router framework. Specifically, we design a 5W Recall Map to classify memory queries into five typical scenarios and define fifteen recall strategy patterns across the corresponding scenarios. We then propose a hierarchical recall tree combined with the Monte Carlo Tree Search algorithm to optimize the selection of strategy and the generation of strategy responses. We construct an instruction tuning dataset and fine-tune multiple open-source large language models (LLMs) to develop MemoCue, an agent that excels in providing memory-inspired responses. Experiments on three representative datasets show that MemoCue surpasses LLM-based methods by 17.74% in recall inspiration. Further human evaluation highlights its advantages in memory-recall applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MemoCue: Empowering LLM-Based Agents for Human Memory Recall via Strategy-Guided Querying" addresses the problem of assisting human memory recall using LLM-based agents. The authors argue that relying solely on retrieving stored memories within the agent is limiting due to storage constraints and incomplete data.  Inspired by the human memory process, they propose a novel Strategy-Guided Recall (SGR) method where the agent proactively guides the user towards recall by transforming the initial user query into a cue-rich one, based on judiciously selected recall strategies. To achieve this, they introduce a Recall Router framework with: a 5W Recall Map for classifying queries into typical forgetting scenarios; a Recall Strategy Pool with corresponding strategies; and a hierarchical recall tree combined with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to optimize strategy selection and response generation. They construct the MemoStrategy dataset to fine-tune LLMs, creating an agent called MemoCue that generates memory-inspired cues.  Experiments on several datasets demonstrate MemoCue's superiority in recall inspiration compared to baseline LLM approaches, and a human evaluation highlights its practical advantages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to agent-assisted memory recall.  The shift from passive memory retrieval to a proactive, strategy-guided query transformation is a significant departure from existing methods. The integration of the 5W model and MCTS for adaptive strategy selection and cue generation is also innovative.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a practical and impactful problem. Improving memory recall has broad applications in everyday life and human-computer interaction. The approach offers a potential solution to the limitations of memory-augmented LLMs in real-world scenarios, where complete memory storage is often infeasible.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and motivates the need for strategy-guided recall.
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The Recall Router framework, incorporating the 5W Recall Map, Strategy Pool, and SGR-MCTS, is well-designed and logically structured.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of MemoCue across multiple datasets, outperforming strong baselines. The human evaluation provides further validation of the approach's real-world utility.
    *   **Address the evaluation gap.** The paper recognized the evaluation limitation and designed a series of evaluation metric for this special memory recall field.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on User Feedback:** The MCTS component relies on simulated user feedback.  The accuracy of this simulation directly impacts the quality of the generated cues. While they address it with exploration factor, the dependence might limit its robustness in a more diverse user population. The fine-grained reward design needs to be evaluated its influence.
    *   **Limited Scope of Strategies:** The predefined set of 15 recall strategies, while grounded in memory theory, may not cover all possible forgetting scenarios. Expanding the strategy pool and exploring methods for dynamic strategy generation could improve the framework's generality.
    *   **Complexity:** The integration of multiple components (5W map, MCTS, LLM fine-tuning) adds complexity to the system. A detailed analysis of the contribution and computational cost of each component is necessary.
    *   **Reproducibility detail.** Though the author provides the model parameters in appendix, more implementation details of the memory datasets are needed.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research in proactive memory assistance and strategy-driven human-computer interaction. The proposed framework and the MemoCue agent offer a promising foundation for developing more intelligent and personalized memory support systems. The idea of strategically guiding users rather than simply retrieving information could be applied to other domains beyond memory recall.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper's novelty, strong empirical results, and practical significance justify a high score. However, the limitations regarding user feedback dependency, limited strategy scope, and complexity prevent it from achieving the highest score. The detailed evaluation metric and reproducibility also need to be improved. The idea is well-executed.
